id: "71499059"
title: "Russian attacks on civilians in the Russo-Ukrainian war (2022–present)"
---

# Russian attacks on civilians in the Russo-Ukrainian war (2022–present)



Aftermath of the Russian [missile strike on an apartment block in Dnipro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Dnipro_residential_building_airstrike) (14 January 2023), which killed 46 civilians

Shelling of the [Kharkiv](2788558.md) Regional Administration

During the [Russo-Ukrainian War (2022–present)](70149799.md), the [Russian military](25709.md) has continuously carried out deliberate attacks against civilian targets[^cite_note-1][^cite_note-2][^cite_note-HRW_Vehicles-3] and [indiscriminate attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiscriminate_attack) in densely populated areas.[^cite_note-HRMMU_Statement_March-4] The [United Nations Human Rights Monitoring Mission in Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Human_Rights_Monitoring_Mission_in_Ukraine) says the Russian military exposed the civilian population to unnecessary and [disproportionate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proportionality_\(law\)#International_humanitarian_law) harm by using [cluster bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cluster_bombs)[^cite_note-UN_treaty_collection-5][^cite_note-HRW_UA_RU_cluster_munition-6][^cite_note-HRMMU_Report_March-7][^cite_note-Wired_Cluster-8] and by firing other weapons with wide-area effects into civilian areas, such as missiles, heavy artillery shells, and multiple launch rockets.[^cite_note-HRMMU_Report_March-7] As of 2026, the attacks had resulted in between the UN-documented 16,000[^cite_note-UNNews9122025-9] and an estimated 40,000 dead civilians.[^cite_note-10] On 22 April 2022, the UN reported that of the 2,343 civilian casualties it had been able to document, it could confirm 92.3% of these deaths were as a result of the actions of the Russian armed forces.[^cite_note-11]&#x20;

On 5 July 2022, the [UN High Commissioner for Human Rights](22463.md) (OHCHR), [Michelle Bachelet](986814.md), reported that most civilian casualties documented by her office had been caused by the Russian army's use of explosive weapons with wide-area effects in populated areas, calling it "indisputable."[^cite_note-OHCHR_July-12] By 30 June 2023, OHCHR assessed that 90.5% of all civilian deaths were caused by such indiscriminate attacks and that 84.2% of them were recorded in Ukrainian-held territory.[^cite_note-OHCHR_July_2023-13] *[El País](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Pa%C3%ADs)* estimated that by March 2023 Russian forces were firing at a rate of between 600,000 and 1.8 million shells per month.[^cite_note-14] By February 2024, Russia had fired between 12 and 17 million artillery shells against Ukraine.[^cite_note-EDM-15] By the end of 2023, Russian forces launched about 7,400 missiles and 3,900 Shahed drone strikes against Ukraine, according to Ukrainian military officials.[^cite_note-16] Reports on the use of cluster bombs raised concerns about the high number of civilian casualties and the long-lasting danger of unexploded ordnance.[^cite_note-HRMMU_Report_March-7][^cite_note-Wired_Cluster-8] According to the OHCHR, cluster bombs have been used by Russian armed forces and [pro-Russian separatists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro-Russian_separatist_forces_in_Donbas) in densely populated areas, resulting in civilian casualties.[^cite_note-Report_OHCHR-17]&#x20;

The deadliest Russian attacks on civilians during the war have been the [Mariupol theatre airstrike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariupol_theatre_airstrike) and the [Bucha massacre](70455343.md), which are estimated to have killed hundreds. Other strikes on civilians with high casualties include the April 2022 [Kramatorsk railway station attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kramatorsk_railway_station_attack) (which killed 63); [3 March 2022 Chernihiv bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3_March_2022_Chernihiv_bombing) (which killed 47); the [July 2022 Chasiv Yar missile strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/July_2022_Chasiv_Yar_missile_strike) (which killed 46), the [Zaporizhzhia civilian convoy attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zaporizhzhia_civilian_convoy_attack) (which killed 32), the [2023 Dnipro residential building airstrike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Dnipro_residential_building_airstrike) (which killed 46), the 2023 [Hroza missile attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hroza_missile_attack) (which killed 59), and the [2025 Sumy airstrike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Sumy_airstrike) (which killed 35 people).[^cite_note-18]&#x20;

In total, Russia has attacked more than 3000 health care targets,[^cite_note-19] more than 4000 schools and universities,[^cite_note-20] more than 2.5 million housing units,[^cite_note-21] more than 700 churches,[^cite_note-22] and killed more than 15,000 civilians as of February 2026.[^cite_note-d689-23]&#x20;

## Chernihiv Oblast



Aftermath of the 3 March Chernihiv bombing

Main article: [Chernihiv strikes (2022–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chernihiv_strikes_\(2022%E2%80%93present\))

Further information: [3 March 2022 Chernihiv bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3_March_2022_Chernihiv_bombing), [16 March 2022 Chernihiv breadline attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/16_March_2022_Chernihiv_breadline_attack), and [August 2023 Chernihiv missile strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/August_2023_Chernihiv_missile_strike)

On 3 March 2022, just after 12\:00 (UTC+2), six Russian [unguided aerial bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unguided_bomb) were filmed falling in a residential area in [Chernihiv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chernihiv).[^cite_note-Independent_Dashcam_captures-24] Analysis by [Amnesty International](18947898.md) found that (at least) eight bombs fell.[^cite_note-Amnesty_UA_Russian_dumb_bomb-25] Two schools (No. 18 and No. 21) and 8 private houses in the intersection between Viacheslava Chornovila and Kruhova streets ([51°30′00″N 31°16′45″E / 51.5001°N 31.2791°E / 51.5001; 31.2791](https://geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Russian_attacks_on_civilians_in_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war_\(2022%E2%80%93present\)\&params=51.5001_N_31.2791_E_dim\:200_region\:UA_type\:event)) were destroyed; 7 more houses were also heavily damaged in the vicinity of Biloruskyi Lane.[^cite_note-26] Local emergency services recorded 38 men and 9 women killed (47 in total) by the bombing and 18 people injured.[^cite_note-Independent_Dashcam_captures-24] As Amnesty International was unable to identify a legitimate military target nearby, it said the attack could be a [war crime](48626.md).[^cite_note-Amnesty_UA_Russian_dumb_bomb-25] [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) (HRW) found no evidence of a "significant \[military] target in or near the intersection when it was hit, ... pointing to a potentially deliberate or reckless, indiscriminate attack. HRW called for an [International Criminal Court](14880.md) investigation and a [United Nations commission of inquiry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_commission_of_inquiry) to decide if a war crime had occurred and to hold to account the people responsible. The HRW investigation included telephone interviews with three witnesses and two other Chernihiv residents and analysis of 22 videos and 12 photographs. The witnesses interviewed by HRW stated that they were unaware of military targets or operations in the neighbourhood.[^cite_note-27] Matilda Bogner, head of the [UN Human Rights Monitoring Mission in Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Human_Rights_Monitoring_Mission_in_Ukraine), stated that the bombing "violated the principles of [distinction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Distinction_\(law\)), of [proportionality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proportionality_\(law\)#International_humanitarian_law), the rule on feasible precautions, and the prohibition of indiscriminate attacks."[^cite_note-HRMMU_Statement_March-4] A bomb crater consistent with a 500 kg bomb was found. [FAB-500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FAB-500) bombs were known to be used during the invasion.[^cite_note-Amnesty_UA_Russian_dumb_bomb-25]&#x20;

On 16 March 2022, a Russian attack killed 14 civilians who were waiting in a line for bread in the city. The event was reported by [Governor of Chernihiv Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_Chernihiv_Oblast) [Vyacheslav Chaus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vyacheslav_Chaus) and the [United States Embassy in Kyiv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_the_United_States,_Kyiv).[^cite_note-28] The incident happened at around 10\:00 [UTC+3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UTC%2B3). Victims of the incident were killed following a blast shot from [heavy artillery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heavy_artillery).[^cite_note-ukr1-29] These civilians were unarmed, and some of them survived the shelling; they were taken to medical facilities by the Chernihiv police.[^cite_note-30] An American citizen was among the dead.[^cite_note-31] Around four hours after the incident, the Chernihiv Regional Prosecutor's Office filed a legal case regarding the attack. The Chernihiv Oblast branch of the [Security Service of Ukraine](709222.md) also started an investigation.[^cite_note-ukr1-29]&#x20;



The Drama Theater building after shelling

On 19 August 2023 at about 11\:30 Saturday morning, seven civilians were killed, dozens hospitalized, and over 150 injured after Russian Armed Forces launched a 9М727 [Iskander](5607520.md) cruise missile and hit the [Taras Shevchenko Music and Drama Theater](https://uk.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%A7%D0%B5%D1%80%D0%BD%D1%96%D0%B3%D1%96%D0%B2%D1%81%D1%8C%D0%BA%D0%B8%D0%B9_%D0%BE%D0%B1%D0%BB%D0%B0%D1%81%D0%BD%D0%B8%D0%B9_%D0%B0%D0%BA%D0%B0%D0%B4%D0%B5%D0%BC%D1%96%D1%87%D0%BD%D0%B8%D0%B9_%D1%83%D0%BA%D1%80%D0%B0%D1%97%D0%BD%D1%81%D1%8C%D0%BA%D0%B8%D0%B9_%D0%BC%D1%83%D0%B7%D0%B8%D1%87%D0%BD%D0%BE-%D0%B4%D1%80%D0%B0%D0%BC%D0%B0%D1%82%D0%B8%D1%87%D0%BD%D0%B8%D0%B9_%D1%82%D0%B5%D0%B0%D1%82%D1%80_%D1%96%D0%BC%D0%B5%D0%BD%D1%96_%D0%A2%D0%B0%D1%80%D0%B0%D1%81%D0%B0_%D0%A8%D0%B5%D0%B2%D1%87%D0%B5%D0%BD%D0%BA%D0%B0) at [Krasna Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krasna_Square), the city center.[^cite_note-32] The detonator of the warhead of the missile was configured for air detonation,[^cite_note-33] so all the dead and the vast majority of the injured were from outside the drama theater.[^cite_note-34] According to the acting mayor of the city, the military exhibition held in the building was the likely target.[^cite_note-35] Early reporting indicated five people killed and 42 injured, including 11 children presumably among the injured. The estimates went up to 90 injured, including 25 hospitalized, and six dead after a six-year-old child died in the hospital and then seven dead after another woman's body was found.[^cite_note-36][^cite_note-37]&#x20;

On 26 December 2025, senators from the US Democratic and Republican parties condemned Russian attacks on Ukrainian civilians gathering to celebrate Christmas, with a Russian drone strike on a multi-story building killing an 80-year-old woman and injuring 10 people, including three children.[^cite_note-x065-38]&#x20;

On 2 March 2026, an 88-year-old woman was reported to have been killed by Russian drones in [Snovsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snovsk). On 12 March, the city council of [Mena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mena,_Ukraine) said that a Russian attack damaging two residential buildings resulted in the death of a 15-year-old girl and her parents being injured.[^cite_note-a674-39]&#x20;

On 17 July 2026, governor Chaus said that Russian drone attacks injured five civilians in the region.[^cite_note-g420-40] Ten days later, the local administration said that a Russian drone had struck an [ATB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATB-Market) supermarket, killing two people including a child, while injuring 20 people.[^cite_note-k779-41]&#x20;

On 26 August 2026, the Bazhaiemo Zdorovia pharmacy chain said that one of its pharmacy assistants and her four-year-old daughter had been killed in a Russian attack on Chernihiv during the morning.[^cite_note-w628-42]&#x20;

## Dnipropetrovsk Oblast

On 7 April 2022, there were three strikes on the oblast during the day. Russian forces struck [Synelnykove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synelnykove_Raion) and [Kryvyi Rih](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kryvyi_Rih_Raion) raions. About four people died and seven were injured. Two civilians also went missing.[^cite_note-ukrinform-43]&#x20;

### Dnipro strikes

Main article: [Dnipro strikes (2022–present)](71335989.md)



Dnipro during a Russian air attack on 26 March 2025



Hospital in Dnipro after Russian missile attack on 26 May 2023. A neighboring veterinary clinic was also destroyed.

On 11 March 2022, three missiles hit the city and killed one person, striking close to an apartment building and a kindergarten.[^cite_note-44] On 28 June, Russian forces fired six [3M-14E Kalibr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3M-54_Kalibr) [cruise missiles](6590.md) from the [Black Sea](3386.md) to Dnipro at around 5\:30 local time. One of them hit an Avtodiesel car repair shop, killing a man and a woman. Seven other people, including a six-year-old boy, were injured. Fragments of the Kalibr missile were found afterwards.[^cite_note-45]&#x20;

On the morning of 29 September 2022, missiles hit residential areas in Dnipro, and three people were killed.[^cite_note-46] The central bus station was also hit.[^cite_note-47] Dnipro was also hit during the 10 October 2022 Russian missile strikes.[^cite_note-48] It was hit by at least five missiles. During the attack that took place during morning rush hour, three civilians were killed.[^cite_note-49]&#x20;

On 18 October 2022, Russian missiles hit energy infrastructure in Dnipro.[^cite_note-50] One man was injured, and a large-scale fire broke out at an energy facility that was severely damaged; more than three dozen residential buildings were damaged, including schools and kindergartens.[^cite_note-51] On 25 October 2022, two people were killed, including a pregnant woman, and four were injured due to a fire at a gas station after fragments of a Russian missile hit it.[^cite_note-52]&#x20;

On 26 November 2022, around noon, a Russian missile strike on Dnipro injured 13 people and partially destroyed seven private houses in Dnipro's [Amur-Nyzhnodniprovskyi District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nyzhniodniprovskyi_District).[^cite_note-53] Dnipro mayor Borys Filatov reported that city communications and infrastructure were not damaged.[^cite_note-54] Governor Valentyn Reznichenko stated that due to the attack, one woman was hospitalised in critical condition.[^cite_note-55] The following day Reznichenko reported that a man was found dead under the rubble.[^cite_note-56] Another Russian missile strike destroyed an enterprise on the night of 29 November 2022; no casualties were reported.[^cite_note-57]&#x20;

On 28 July 2025, a Russian missile strike on [Kamianske](1261408.md) damaged multiple buildings, including a maternity hospital. Two civilians were killed and five were wounded, including two women critically, one of them pregnant.[^cite_note-k348-58]&#x20;

On 29 April 2025, regional governor Lysak said that Russia had launched a mass [drone](38212231.md) attack on Dnipro using Shahed drones, setting several homes on fire and killing one 53-year-old man.[^cite_note-q993-59][^cite_note-s456-60]&#x20;

On 24 March 2026, the head of the Dnipropetrovsk Oblast Military Administration, Oleksandr Hanzha, said that nine people had been injured by a Russian drone striking a 14-storey building in Dnipro, including an 18-month-old boy.[^cite_note-l827-61]&#x20;

On 14 April 2026, Hanzha said that a Russian attack during the morning on the central Dnipropetrovsk region killed five people and wounded 27.[^cite_note-j511-62] On 23 April, Hanzha reported another attack, with Russian drones killing two people and wounding 10, with two young girls aged nine and 14 being among the wounded as the attack damaged infrastructure, including a 13-story residential building.[^cite_note-u771-63]&#x20;

On 17 July 2026, Hanzha said that 73-year-old man had been injured in a Russian attack.[^cite_note-g420-40]&#x20;

On 1 August 2026, the Dnipropetrovsk Oblast Prosecutor's Office said that two people were killed and 12 injured in Russian attacks, which included an [FPV drone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First-person_view_\(remote_control\)) striking a passenger bus.[^cite_note-l749-64]&#x20;

On 5 September 2026, Hanzha reported Russian attacks across six districts, resulting in one person being killed and five wounded.[^cite_note-s403-65]&#x20;

#### Dnipro apartment block strike



Aftermath of the missile strike on a Dnipro apartment block, January 2023

Main article: [2023 Dnipro residential building airstrike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Dnipro_residential_building_airstrike)

On 14 January 2023 at about 3\:30 p.m., a Russian Kh-22 type missile hit a nine-story residential building in Dnipro on [Naberezhna Peremohy St](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Naberezhna_Peremohy_St\&action=edit\&redlink=1), [Sobornyi District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sobornyi_District,_Dnipro) in the right-bank part of the city, destroying one entrance and 236 apartments.[^cite_note-66] On 19 January, the official casualty rate was stated as 46 people killed, 80 injured (12 in critical condition), and 11 people reported missing.[^cite_note-67] 14 children were reported among the injured, and 39 inhabitants were rescued.[^cite_note-68] The destruction left about 400 people homeless.[^cite_note-69] This strike became the most destructive Russian attack on a residential building in Ukraine in the last six months.[^cite_note-70] A three-day period of mourning was declared in Dnipro.[^cite_note-71]&#x20;

#### February 2026 drone strike on bus carrying mineworkers

On 1 February 2026, a Russian drone struck a bus carrying mineworkers, killing at least a dozen people and injuring several others, according to Ukrainian emergency services. Ukrainian Energy Minister [Denys Shmyhal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denys_Shmyhal) called the attack a "cynical and targeted attack on energy sector workers." [DTEK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DTEK), the company that owns the bus, accused Russia of also carrying out a "large-scale terrorist attack" on its mines.[^cite_note-h961-72][^cite_note-s489-73]&#x20;

#### June 2026 bombardment

On 27 June 2026, Dnipropetrovsk's regional head Oleksandr Ganja said two districts in the region had been attack ″more than 30 times with drones and aerial bombs″, killing one person.[^cite_note-t265-74]&#x20;

#### August 2026 attack killing three-month-old

On 15 August 2026, Hanzha said that Russian attack drones struck a residential building in [Marhanets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marhanets), killing a three-month-old boy and injuring 11 people, seven being hospitalized in severe condition. Among the injured were also two children, a five-year-old boy and 12-year-old girl.[^cite_note-y750-75][^cite_note-q120-76]&#x20;

### Kryvyi Rih missile strikes

Main article: [Kryvyi Rih strikes (2022–present)](72034098.md)



Aftermath of Russian missile attack on Kryvyi Rih on 4 April 2025, which killed 20 people, including nine children

Russian missiles struck residential buildings in the southern central city of [Kryvyi Rih](624764.md), Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelenskyy's hometown, several times. On 13 June 2023, Russian cruise missiles struck an apartment block and civilian warehouses in Kryvyi Rih,[^cite_note-77] killing thirteen people.[^cite_note-78] On 31 July 2023, a Russian missile struck an apartment block in Kryvyi Rih, killing at least six civilians, including a 10-year-old girl and her mother.[^cite_note-79]&#x20;

On 28 August 2024, local authorities said that Kryvyi Rih was struck by a Russian missile, which hit civilian infrastructure. Eight people were reported to have been wounded by the attack. As it was struck, Kryvyi Rih was observing an official day of mourning for an attack the previous day that had killed four civilians in a hotel.[^cite_note-h046-80]&#x20;

On 28 October 2024, a Russian missile struck a residential building in Kryvyi Rih, killing one person and wounding at least 11.[^cite_note-z950-81]&#x20;

On 6 December 2024, two people were killed by a Russian missile strike on an administrative building. Emergency services added that residential houses had also been damaged and 19 people, including a child, had been injured in the attack.[^cite_note-b667-82]&#x20;

On 12 March 2025, Dnipropetrovsk regional governor [Serhiy Lysak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serhiy_Lysak) said that a Russian missile attack killed a woman and injured at least nine others, the attack also damaging several buildings, including an educational facility and high-rise apartments.[^cite_note-b295-83]&#x20;

On 2 April 2025, [Ukrainska Pravda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainska_Pravda) reported that Russia had struck Kryvyi Rih with a [ballistic missile](38025.md). The attack damaged multi-story residential buildings, a gym, and an administrative building. According to the local military administration, four people were killed and 14 were injured, including two children.[^cite_note-o879-84][^cite_note-e228-85]&#x20;

See also: [2025 Kryvyi Rih playground strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Kryvyi_Rih_playground_strike)

On 4 April 2025, Russia struck a dense residential area with a missile, spraying shrapnel which also [hit a playground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Kryvyi_Rih_playground_strike). The attack killed 20 people, 11 adults and nine children, and dozens of people were injured. According to Reuters, the strike, described as one of the deadliest attacks on civilians in months, jeopardised the US's push for a ceasefire in the war.[^cite_note-p808-86][^cite_note-k464-87][^cite_note-r631-88][^cite_note-q827-89] The United Nations called the strike Russia's deadliest attack on Ukrainian children in the three years since the invasion started.[^cite_note-g079-90]&#x20;

In January 2026, the head of the Kryvyi Rih Defense Council, [Oleksandr Vilkul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleksandr_Vilkul), said that a combined Russian missile and drone attack had struck a residential area, injuring 17 people, including three children and eight women.[^cite_note-t425-91]&#x20;

On 30 July 2026, Ukrainian officials said that a Russian missile attack destroyed a residential house in [Radushne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radushne), killing five members of the same family, both parents and three of their children, while two children survived and were pulled from the rubble. A total of two homes were reported destroyed in the attack, with eight people also being injured, including an 84-year-old woman.[^cite_note-v196-92][^cite_note-q877-93]&#x20;

#### August 2026 Kryvyi Rih shopping center double-tap strike

On 21 August 2026, Ukrainian officials said that a Russian [double-tap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Double-tap_strike) drone strike on a shopping center killed 16 people while injuring 130, including 23 children. Officials said that a second drone deliberately hit as emergency workers responded to the scene, with Zelenskyy calling the attack a ″terrorist act″.[^cite_note-k025-94]&#x20;

### Chaplyne railway station strike



Aftermath of the Chaplyne railway station strike

Main article: [Chaplyne railway station attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaplyne_railway_station_attack)

On 24 August 2022, the [Independence Day of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Day_of_Ukraine), Russian forces struck [Chaplyne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaplyne), damaging a railway station, a utility building, and a residential neighborhood.[^cite_note-\:WaPochaplyne-95] Several passenger rail cars were set on fire and destroyed.[^cite_note-\:WaPochaplyne-95] Ukrainian sources described multiple rockets or missiles being used in several attacks.[^cite_note-96] At least 25 people (including 2 children) died and about 31 were injured.[^cite_note-97][^cite_note-98] The Russian defense ministry claimed it had targeted a military train using a single [Iskander missile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iskander_missile) and that the attack had killed 200 Ukrainian soldiers.[^cite_note-99] According to an [Associated Press](18935732.md) reporter on site, there was no visible indication that Ukrainian soldiers were among the victims.[^cite_note-\:3-100] International legal experts said that the attack might qualify as a war crime if civilians were the target.[^cite_note-\:3-100]&#x20;

### Attacks on civilian transport

On 16 September 2026, *ABC News* reported that a Russian drone striking a passenger bus had killed five people and wounded at least seven in [Nikopol](1958336.md).[^cite_note-q574-101]&#x20;

## Donetsk Oblast

See also: [Russian occupation of Donetsk Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_occupation_of_Donetsk_Oblast)

There were at least 7,395 civilian casualties (of which 3,511 were killed) in the government-controlled territory of Donetsk and Luhansk oblasts and 2,035 civilian casualties (of which 467 were killed) in the territory controlled by the Russian forces and affiliated groups between February and November 2022.[^cite_note-102] The months-long siege of Mariupol caused a large number of civilian casualties, and a few other attacks with many civilian casualties occurred in the oblast. Donetsk itself experienced several attacks with civilian casualties, including the [shelling of a market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maisky_Market_attack) and the [mayor's office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_2022_Donetsk_attack). Pro-Russian officials blamed Ukraine for the attacks, while Ukraine blamed Russia or did not comment on the attacks.[^cite_note-103][^cite_note-104]&#x20;

On 8 August 2022, the UK Ministry of Defence issued a statement accusing Russia of using anti-personnel [PFM-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PFM-1) scatterable anti-personnel mines along the defensive lines in Donbas, adding that such mines are indiscriminate and pose a threat to civilians.[^cite_note-105]&#x20;

### Vuhledar cluster bomb attack

On 24 February 2022, 10\:30 (UTC), [Vuhledar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vuhledar) was attacked with a [9M79 Tochka missile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OTR-21_Tochka), which landed next to a hospital and killed four civilians and injured ten.[^cite_note-Report_OHCHR-17]<sup>: para. 27–30 </sup> Amnesty International described it as "irrefutable evidence of violations of international humanitarian law and international human rights law" by Russian forces.[^cite_note-106] Human Rights Watch (HRW) found that the Vuhledar hospital attack used a 9N123 cluster munition. The 9N123 contains fifty [9N24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9N24) individual submunitions, which each split into 316 bomblets. HRW based its analysis on contacts with the hospital and municipal administrations and multiple pieces of photographic evidence. HRW called for Russian forces to stop making "unlawful attacks with weapons that indiscriminately kill and maim".[^cite_note-HRW_UA_RU_cluster_munition-6] The press secretary of the Russian Federation, Dmitry Peskov, denied Russian involvement, saying that this type of ammunition is used by the [Armed Forces of Ukraine](42799055.md).[^cite_note-107]&#x20;

### Lyman cluster bomb attack

Main article: [Lyman cluster bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyman_cluster_bombing)



Damage from the cluster bombing in Lyman

On 8 July 2023, Russian [cluster bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cluster_bombs) launched by a [BM-30 Smerch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BM-30_Smerch) struck a residential area of [Lyman](6862925.md) in Donetsk Oblast. Nine civilians were killed and nineteen wounded. Witnesses said residents "had gathered to trade dairy products and fresh produce from their gardens, as they did most mornings." The use of cluster bombs in areas with civilians "makes an attack indiscriminate, in violation of [international humanitarian law](1093604.md)."[^cite_note-108]&#x20;

### Siege of Mariupol

Main article: [Siege of Mariupol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Mariupol)



Destroyed residential buildings in Mariupol, 2022



Ukrainian civilians killed by Russian shelling in Mariupol

Between 1 and 2 March 2022, Russian artillery reportedly shelled a densely populated neighbourhood in the city for nearly 15 hours, causing significant destruction. Deputy mayor Sergei Orlov reported that "at least hundreds of people \[were] dead".[^cite_note-gunter-109] On 16 March, the [Institute for the Study of War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_for_the_Study_of_War) (ISW) reported that Russian forces continued to commit [war crimes](48626.md) in Mariupol, including "targeting civilian infrastructure".[^cite_note-110] On 18 March, Lieutenant General [James Hockenhull](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Hockenhull), Chief of Defence Intelligence for the United Kingdom (UK), described "continued targeting of civilians in Mariupol".[^cite_note-111] As of 20 March, local authorities have estimated that at least 2,300 people were killed during the siege.[^cite_note-112]&#x20;

On 20 March 2022, Ukrainian authorities announced that Russian troops had [bombed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariupol_art_school_bombing) an art school in the city where hundreds (about 400) were sheltering.[^cite_note-113] The [Mariupol City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariupol_City_Council) made the announcement through the instant messaging service [Telegram](41501305.md), highlighting that many of those sheltering in the school were women, children and elderly.[^cite_note-114] However, Petro Andryushchenko, an advisor to the mayor of Mariupol, raised the concern that there was no exact number on how many people were using the school as a refuge.[^cite_note-115]&#x20;

By 18 April, Ukrainian officials estimated that at least 95% of Mariupol had been destroyed in the fighting, largely as a result of the Russian bombing campaigns.[^cite_note-hasn't_surrendered-116] City officials reported that up to 21,000 civilians had been killed.[^cite_note-10,000_civilians_killed-117][^cite_note-21,000_civilians_killed-118] On 16 June, the [UN High Commissioner for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_High_Commissioner_for_Human_Rights) said that evidence strongly suggests the Russian armed forces committed serious violations of international humanitarian and human rights law in Mariupol, including the shelling and rocket attacks that destroyed much of the city.[^cite_note-119][^cite_note-120] In a separate statement, Human Rights Watch said Russia's military tactics were [indiscriminate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiscriminate_attack) and caused a disproportionate effect on the civilians in the city. It also warned that going forward, access to the city and preservation of evidence were likely to be issues, given Russia's occupation of the city, and it called for international accountability.[^cite_note-121]&#x20;

Independent media and the Ukrainian side accused the Russian military of bombing civilian shelters with cluster munitions during shelling of the city and attacks on Azovstal.[^cite_note-122] According to the mayor of Mariupol, at the end of April, during the two-month siege of the city by Russian troops, more than 20 thousand people died—twice as many as during the 2-year occupation of the city during World War II.[^cite_note-123] Oksana Pokalchuk, director of Amnesty International Ukraine, said they were able to prove the use of cluster munitions by Russia after interviewing a victim who provided them with a fragment of ammunition that was removed from his thigh.[^cite_note-124]&#x20;

#### Targeting of humanitarian corridors

During the shelling of Mariupol by Russian forces, a number of attempts to establish a [humanitarian evacuation corridor](35072890.md) to evacuate civilians from the city were made but failed when the corridor was targeted by Russian forces.[^cite_note-125] On 5 March, a five-hour ceasefire was declared, but evacuations were quickly halted after shelling continued during the declared time.[^cite_note-126] The next day, the [International Committee of the Red Cross](5023832.md) (ICRC) announced that a second attempt to establish an evacuation corridor had failed.[^cite_note-127][^cite_note-128]&#x20;

#### Mariupol hospital airstrike



Aftermath of the Mariupol hospital airstrike

Main article: [Mariupol hospital airstrike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariupol_hospital_airstrike)

On 9 March 2022, [Children's and Maternity Hospital No. 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_hospitals_in_Ukraine), a hospital complex functioning both as a children's [hospital](18994221.md) and maternity ward,[^cite_note-Meduza_definitely_not_staged-129] was bombed several times by Russian forces during a ceasefire, killing at least four people and injuring at least seventeen, also leading to at least one stillbirth[^cite_note-Telegraph_atrocity_as_maternity_hospital-130] Ukrainian authorities described the damage to the hospital as "colossal." Video footage following the attacks showed "much of the front of the building ... ripped away" and "mangled cars burning outside."[^cite_note-AJE_RU_bombing_childrens_hospital-131] Hospital wards were "reduced to a wreckage, walls \[had] collapsed, rubble cover\[ed] medical equipment, windows \[were] blown out, and shattered glass \[was] everywhere."[^cite_note-132] On 10 March, local authorities stated that one girl and two other people had been killed in the bombing, one of whom was a woman at a late stage of pregnancy; neither she nor her unborn child survived.[^cite_note-Euronews_RU_attack_Mariupol_hospital-133] Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelenskyy stated that people had "hidden" from the attack in time, minimising the number of casualties.[^cite_note-Ukrinform_beyond_atrocities-134]&#x20;

Deputy Mayor of Mariupol, Sergei Orlov, stated, "We don't understand how it is possible in modern life to bomb \[a] children's hospital."[^cite_note-Telegraph_atrocity_as_maternity_hospital-130] The Mariupol City Council described the bombing by Russian aircraft as deliberate.[^cite_note-Telegraph_atrocity_as_maternity_hospital-130] Zelenskyy claimed that the attack constituted "proof that the [genocide](12441.md) of Ukrainians \[was] taking place."[^cite_note-Ukrinform_beyond_atrocities-134] Sergei Orlov, deputy mayor of Mariupol, described the attack as both a war crime and genocide.[^cite_note-Guardian_children_under_rubble-135] [British prime minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_prime_minister) [Boris Johnson](19065069.md) described the attack as "depraved."[^cite_note-AJE_RU_bombing_childrens_hospital-131] [Jen Psaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jen_Psaki), press secretary of United States President Joe Biden, stated that "It is horrifying to see the ... barbaric use of military force to go after innocent civilians in a sovereign country."[^cite_note-AJE_RU_bombing_childrens_hospital-131] Josep Borrell, High Representative of the European Union for Foreign Affairs and Security Policy, described the bombing as a "heinous war crime".[^cite_note-Euronews_RU_attack_Mariupol_hospital-133] [Cardinal Secretary of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardinal_Secretary_of_State) of the [Vatican City](32408.md) [Pietro Parolin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pietro_Parolin) expressed dismay at the bombing, calling it an "unacceptable attack on civilians."[^cite_note-136] António Guterres, Secretary-General of the United Nations, wrote that the attack was "horrific" and that "this senseless violence must stop."[^cite_note-137]&#x20;

On 10 March, the Russian Minister of Foreign Affairs and the Ministry of Defence publicly claimed that the bombing was justified. According to *[Ukrainska Pravda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainska_Pravda)*, foreign minister [Sergey Lavrov](541346.md) confirmed that the bombing of the hospital was a deliberate action. He stated, "A few days ago, at a UN Security Council meeting, the Russian delegation presented factual information that this maternity hospital had long been taken over by the Azov Battalion and other radicals and that all the women in labour, all the nurses and in general all the staff had been told to leave it. It was a base of the ultra-radical Azov Battalion."[^cite_note-UAPravda_Lavrov_confirms-138] On 10 March 2022, [Twitter](9988187.md) removed a tweet from the [Russian embassy in the UK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_Russia,_London) which claimed that the Mariupol hospital attack was "fake" and that Marianna Vyshegirskaya, one of the victims, was an "actress" by citing her blogging career, as a violation of Twitter rules. British politicians welcomed the move and accused the Russian embassy of [disinformation](142690.md).[^cite_note-139] *[Meduza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meduza_\(website\))* stated that the Russian representative to the United Nations, [Vasily Nebenzya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vasily_Nebenzya), had on 7 March referred to *Maternity Hospital No. 1* ([47°08′20″N 37°36′12″E / 47.13892°N 37.60337°E / 47.13892; 37.60337](https://geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Russian_attacks_on_civilians_in_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war_\(2022%E2%80%93present\)\&params=47.13892_N_37.60337_E_dim\:200_region\:UA)) as a hospital that he claimed was used by Ukrainian armed forces as a firing point, not *Maternity Hospital No. 3*. *Meduza* described Lavrov as having confused *Hospital No. 1*, referred to by Nebenzya, with the hospital that was bombed, *Hospital No. 3*.[^cite_note-Meduza_definitely_not_staged-129] On 22 March 2022, Russian journalist [Alexander Nevzorov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Nevzorov) was charged under Russia's ["fake news" law](70646883.md) after he published information about the Russian shelling of a maternity hospital in Mariupol.[^cite_note-140] Under a new law passed on 4 March, he could be sentenced to up to 15 years in prison.[^cite_note-141]&#x20;

#### Mariupol theatre airstrike

Main article: [Mariupol theatre airstrike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariupol_theatre_airstrike)



[Mariupol theatre airstrike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariupol_theatre_airstrike) conducted by the Russian Armed Forces on 16 March 2022

On 16 March, Ukraine accused Russian forces of shelling civilian areas in Mariupol. Artillery hit numerous locations, including a [swimming pool](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swimming_pool) building and a vehicle convoy; shelling then struck the [Donetsk Regional Drama Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donetsk_Regional_Drama_Theatre) that was being used as an [air raid shelter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_raid_shelter). With a large number of civilians inside, the building was reduced to rubble.[^cite_note-142] The bomb shelter in the basement of the theatre survived the bombing, but many people were still trapped underneath the burning rubble. A member of the Ukrainian parliament from Mariupol, Dmytro Gurin, said that the rescue efforts were hampered due to continued attacks on the area by Russian forces.[^cite_note-143]&#x20;

By 17 March, the number of casualties was unclear; some emerged alive.[^cite_note-144] By 18 March, around 130 survivors had been rescued.[^cite_note-145] The Mariupol City Council stated that according to initial information, no one had been killed, although one person was gravely wounded.[^cite_note-146] On 25 March, the council estimated that about 300 people had been killed as a result of the airstrike.[^cite_note-147] On 4 May, Associated Press published an investigation with evidence pointing to 600 dead in the airstrike. Many survivors estimated around 200 people—including rescuers—escaping through the main exit or one side entrance; the other side and the back were crushed.[^cite_note-ap_2022-05-04-148] Estimates of civilian deaths vary, ranging from at least a dozen (Amnesty International)[^cite_note-Amnesty_Mariupol_theatre-149] to 600 (Associated Press).[^cite_note-ap_2022-05-04-148] At least eleven are confirmed by a Russian source.[^cite_note-150]&#x20;

Ukraine accused the Russian Armed Forces of deliberately bombing the theatre while it was sheltering civilians.[^cite_note-151] Russia first claimed that the reason the theatre was bombed was because it was "being used as a base by the Ukrainian military", and then denied the allegations and instead accused the Azov Battalion of blowing up the building, Both Russian claims have been refuted by independent investigation.[^cite_note-152] The theatre is among the many [Ukrainian heritage and cultural sites destroyed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_cultural_heritage_during_the_2022_Russian_invasion) during the invasion. The attack was classified as a war crime by the Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe[^cite_note-153] and Amnesty International.[^cite_note-Amnesty_Mariupol_theatre-149]&#x20;

### Kramatorsk railway station attack

Main article: [Kramatorsk railway station attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kramatorsk_railway_station_attack)



Aftermath of the Kramatorsk railway station attack, April 2022

On 8 April 2022, at 10\:24 and 10\:25, media affiliated with the [Donetsk People's Republic](42425747.md) published videos showing a pair of missiles being launched from [Shakhtarsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shakhtarsk), a city under [separatist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_separatist_forces_in_Donbas) control.[^cite_note-Meduzakramatorsk-154] At approximately 10\:30, two missiles hit near the railway station building in [Kramatorsk](2864798.md),[^cite_note-KramatorskBBC-155] and the first reports were published in Ukrainian media at around 10\:45.[^cite_note-Meduzakramatorsk-154] According to the Ukrainian government, between 1000 and 4000 civilians, mainly women and children, were present at the station awaiting evacuation from the region, which was being subjected to heavy Russian shelling.[^cite_note-156] the attack left at least 60 dead and 110+ wounded.[^cite_note-Report_OHCHR-17]<sup>: para. 32 </sup> The missiles were initially misidentified as [Iskander](5607520.md) [ballistic missiles](38025.md).[^cite_note-157] [Pavlo Kyrylenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pavlo_Kyrylenko), governor of Donetsk oblast, later specified that they had rather been [Tochka-U](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OTR-21_Tochka) missiles armed with cluster munitions.[^cite_note-KramatorskBBC-155] The remnants of one of the missiles had the Russian words ЗА ДЕТЕЙ (*za detey*), meaning "\[in revenge] for the children", painted in white on its outside.[^cite_note-158] It also bore serial number Ш91579, which investigators said could potentially help trace it back to its original arsenal.[^cite_note-159]&#x20;

Initially, Russian state media and pro-Russian telegram channels claimed successful Russian airstrikes on a military transport target in Kramatorsk.[^cite_note-160] However, after it became clear that the missiles had killed civilians, earlier reports were redacted; the Russian government also denied responsibility for the attack, and the Russian Ministry of Defence characterized it as a "Ukrainian hoax."[^cite_note-161] The Russian Ministry of Defence later claimed that the missiles were launched by Ukrainian forces from the city of [Dobropillia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dobropillia), southwest of Kramatorsk.[^cite_note-162] Russian media also said that the serial number of the missile was in the same range as one used by Ukrainian forces; however, these claims were rapidly debunked.[^cite_note-163] A fake video clip with a mock BBC logo, attributing blame to the Ukrainian forces, circulated through pro-Russian telegram channels and Russian state television since 10 April. However, the BBC said that it has not produced any such video.[^cite_note-164] The Russian Ministry of Defense claimed that their forces no longer use Tochka-U missiles. However, Amnesty International had published videos about the use of Tochka-U missiles in other cities before Kramatorsk.[^cite_note-165] investigators from the open-source [Belarusian Hajun project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belarusian_Hajun_project) had also published videos of several Russian trucks with Tochka missiles heading from Belarus to Ukraine with 'V' markings on 5 and 30 March.[^cite_note-166] The Institute for the Study of War assessed that the Russian [8th Guards Combined Arms Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/8th_Guards_Combined_Arms_Army), which is active in the [Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donbas) area, is equipped with Tochka-U missiles.[^cite_note-167]&#x20;

[Michelle Bachelet](986814.md), the United Nations High Commissioner for Human Rights;[^cite_note-168] [Dunja Mijatović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dunja_Mijatovi%C4%87); the [Council of Europe Commissioner for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe_Commissioner_for_Human_Rights);[^cite_note-169] Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelenskyy;[^cite_note-170] [European Commission](9974.md) [President](24468.md) [Ursula von der Leyen](2991932.md);[^cite_note-171] French Foreign Minister [Jean-Yves Le Drian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Yves_Le_Drian);[^cite_note-172] [British Defence Secretary](302332.md) [Ben Wallace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Wallace_\(politician\));[^cite_note-KramatorskAP-173] United Nations Secretary-General António Guterres[^cite_note-KramatorskAP-173] and [Oleksandr Kamyshin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleksandr_Kamyshin), chairman of [Ukrainian Railways](3149341.md),[^cite_note-174] described the event as "war crimes" and "a crime against humanity" by Russian forces. The Security Service of Ukraine opened criminal proceedings under Article 438 of the Criminal Code.[^cite_note-175] [Royal United Services Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_United_Services_Institute) analyst Justin Bronk said that Russia aimed to damage Ukrainian transport infrastructure to make it difficult for Ukrainian forces to move around Donbas. He also suggested that Russia opted for the Tochka-U missile type due to its use by the Ukrainian army, in order to "muddy the waters."[^cite_note-KramatorskAP-173] [The Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon) highlighted Russian responsibility for the attack, as well as the strategic importance of the railway junction.[^cite_note-176]&#x20;

### Kramatorsk restaurant strike

Main article: [Kramatorsk restaurant attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kramatorsk_restaurant_attack)



Aftermath of the Kramatorsk restaurant strike, June 2023

On 27 June 2023, Russian forces attacked a pizza restaurant in [Kramatorsk](2864798.md) with an [Iskander missile](5607520.md), killing 13 people, including four children and Ukrainian writer [Victoria Amelina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Amelina). About sixty people were wounded.[^cite_note-177]&#x20;

Kramatorsk was about 18 miles (30 km) from the eastern frontline.[^cite_note-Guardian_Kramatorsk_restaurant-178] The restaurant was popular with local civilians; war correspondents; humanitarian volunteers; as well as Ukrainian soldiers on leave to 'rest and recuperate' and meet their loved ones.[^cite_note-179] Several journalists were having a meal when the missile struck, including three Colombians.[^cite_note-Guardian_Kramatorsk_restaurant-178] Russia's Defense Ministry claimed the strike targeted a "temporary command post" of the Ukrainian army. Ukraine's prosecutor general said the missile has [high precision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circular_error_probable), "which means Russia was deliberately targeting civilians."[^cite_note-Guardian_Kramatorsk_restaurant-178] Ukraine's [Security Service](709222.md) later arrested a local man who allegedly helped coordinate the strike with the Russian military.[^cite_note-Guardian_Kramatorsk_restaurant-178]&#x20;

### July 2025 Kramatorsk residential building strike

On 22 July 2025, a Russian aerial bomb hit a residential apartment building in Kramatorsk, injuring at least eight civilians and killing a 10-year-old boy.[^cite_note-t029-180]&#x20;

### Chasiv Yar missile strike



Aftermath of the Chasiv Yar rocket strike

Main article: [Chasiv Yar missile strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chasiv_Yar_missile_strike)

On 9 July 2022, a missile strike on two residential buildings in [Chasiv Yar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chasiv_Yar) was carried out by the Russian Armed Forces[^cite_note-181] at 21\:17 local time. At least 48 people were killed. Due to the impact, a five-story residential building partially collapsed. Two entrances were completely destroyed.[^cite_note-182] The strike was alleged, including by [Donetsk Oblast](494889.md) governor Pavlo Kyrylenko,[^cite_note-183] to have been performed with "Uragan," a self-propelled 220 mm multiple rocket launcher designed in the Soviet Union.[^cite_note-svoboda-184] The Russian Defense Ministry claimed that they destroyed a "temporary deployment point" of a Ukrainian territorial defence unit.[^cite_note-185] As of 10 July, 67 rescue workers of the [State Emergency Service of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Emergency_Service_of_Ukraine) were trying to help the victims[^cite_note-186] and more than 20 people were still feared to be trapped under the rubble.[^cite_note-reuters-187] Rescue and search operations continued until the morning of 14 July 2022. Rescuers dismantled about 525 tons of destroyed elements of the building. 323 employees of the State Emergency Service and 9 units of equipment were involved.[^cite_note-188] As of 13 July 48 dead were found under the rubble of the building,[^cite_note-reuters_2022_07_12-189] and nine wounded were rescued as of 12 July.[^cite_note-svoboda-184] A local resident told The New York Times that there were 10 elderly civilians in the buildings, but that members of the military had come to lodge there two days earlier. Two soldiers who probably took turns sleeping in the building after being on duty were among the dead.[^cite_note-190] Andriy Yermak, the chief of staff to Ukraine's president, said that the strike was "another terrorist attack" and that Russia should be designated a "state sponsor of terrorism" as a result.[^cite_note-191] Russian military spokesman [Igor Konashenkov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Igor_Konashenkov) stated that Russia had killed "over 300 nationalists" in an attack on Chasiv Yar, but did not specify whether or not they were referring to the July airstrike or an earlier attack.[^cite_note-AFPkharkiv-192]&#x20;

### Donetsk City

See also: [Maisky Market attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maisky_Market_attack), [March 2022 Donetsk attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_2022_Donetsk_attack), and [September 2022 Donetsk attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_2022_Donetsk_attack)

On 27 July 2022, thousands of miniature plastic antipersonnel [PFM-1 mines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PFM-1_mine) were dispersed over civilian areas of the Russian held city of [Donetsk](562951.md) by a [BM-27 Uragan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BM-27_Uragan) multiple rocket launcher. This resulted in a number of people being injured, most prominently [Semen Pegov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semen_Pegov), a Russian [war blogger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warblog) calling himself "War Gonzo."[^cite_note-193] The Russian Foreign Ministry and local sources accused the Ukrainian army of being responsible for their deployment. The so-called "butterfly mines" are banned under the 1997 Ottawa Treaty (signed by Ukraine but not by Russia) due to the high danger they pose to civilians.[^cite_note-194][^cite_note-195][^cite_note-196]&#x20;

### Sloviansk missile strike



Damage from the April 2023 Sloviansk missile strike

Main article: [April 2023 Sloviansk airstrike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April_2023_Sloviansk_airstrike)

On 14 April 2023, on [Orthodox Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Orthodoxy) [Good Friday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Good_Friday), Russian forces launched eight [S-300](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S-300_missile_system) missiles at [Sloviansk](2533448.md). One struck a five-story residential building, killing 15 civilians, including a child, and wounding 24.[^cite_note-197]&#x20;

### Kostiantynivka shopping centre missile attack

Main article: [2024 Kostiantynivka supermarket strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Kostiantynivka_supermarket_strike)

On 9 August 2024, a [Kostiantynivka](2879020.md) shopping centre was struck by a Russian air-to-surface missile in the middle of the day. Ukrainian authorities reported at least 14 deaths and 43 injuries.[^cite_note-i773-198]&#x20;

### Pokrovsk missiles strikes

Main articles: [2023 Pokrovsk missile strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Pokrovsk_missile_strike) and [2024 Pokrovsk missile strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Pokrovsk_missile_strike)

### Dobropillia airstrikes

On 8 March 2025, the Ukrainian Interior Ministry and Donetsk Oblast Governor [Vadym Filashkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vadym_Filashkin) said that Russian missile and drone attacks on [Dobropillia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dobropillia) had killed 11 people and injured 47, with children being among five of the dead and seven of the injured. The attack took place shortly after US president [Donald Trump](4848272.md) halted intelligence sharing with Ukraine, causing concerns that Russian missiles could now strike cities without prior warning.[^cite_note-w133-199][^cite_note-n958-200]&#x20;

### Yarova airstrike

Main article: [2025 Yarova attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Yarova_attack)



Victims of those killed in the Yarova airstrike

On 9 September 2025, officials said a Russian guided bomb struck the village of [Yarova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yarova), killing 24 elderly people who were collecting their pensions in the village and wounding another 19 people.[^cite_note-o139-201][^cite_note-n864-202]&#x20;

### 2025 Christmas attack

On 26 December 2025, senators from the US Democratic and Republican parties condemned Russian attacks on Ukrainian civilians gathering to celebrate Christmas, with a Russian strike in Donetsk killing a volunteer from Kharkiv.[^cite_note-x065-38]&#x20;

### Shelling of Druzhkivka

On 4 February 2026, Donetsk officials said that Russian forces shelled a [Druzhkivka](6862714.md) market during busy hours, killing seven people and wounding 15 others. Donetsk governor Filashkin called the attack, which took place as Ukrainian and Russian officials were taking part in US-brokered negotiations, a war crime and said that cluster munitions were used for the attack.[^cite_note-j041-203][^cite_note-x905-204]&#x20;

On 12 July 2026, the ″12 Vartovykh″ (12 Guards) charity said that a volunteer who had rescued hundreds of animals from dangerous areas was killed by a Russian guided aerial bomb in Druzhkivka.[^cite_note-y101-205]&#x20;

### July 2026 airstrikes on residential infrastructure

On 10 July 2026, regional officials said that Russia carried out seven airstrikes on Kramatorsk and [Bilenke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bilenke,_Donetsk_Oblast) striking residential houses and apartments and killing four civilians, including two siblings aged 14 and 18, and injuring nine people. A war crime investigation was launched by the Donetsk Oblast Prosecutor's Office.[^cite_note-n700-206]&#x20;

On 17 July, Filashkin said that one person was killed in Nadiia, while Russian attacks also injured 14 people across the Donetsk region, most of them in Kramatorsk.[^cite_note-g420-40]&#x20;

### August 2026 aerial bomb strike

On 31 August 2026, Vadym Liakh, the head of Sloviansk's city administration, said that four people had been injured when Russia struck a residential building with a FAB-500 aerial bomb. The Donetsk Oblast Prosecutor's Office said that the bomb was equipped with a [guidance kit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UMPK_\(bomb_kit\)), with the strike damaging 34 apartment blocks and houses.[^cite_note-z976-207]&#x20;

### September 2026 Kramatorsk shelling

On 20 September 2026, Kramatorsk officials said one person had been killed by repeated Russian shelling.[^cite_note-d109-208]&#x20;

## Bombardment of Izium



Destroyed apartment blocks in Izium

See also: [Battle of Izium (2022)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Izium_\(2022\)) and [Izium mass graves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Izium_mass_graves)

On 3 March 2022, Russian forces bombed the central hospital in [Izium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Izium). Eight people died, and the hospital sustained "significant damage."[^cite_note-209] On 8 March, the same recently refurbished hospital in the city was destroyed during shelling,[^cite_note-210][^cite_note-211] this was followed on 11 March by an attack to a psychiatric hospital.[^cite_note-212] On 15 September 2022, after Ukrainian forces had retaken Izium, several mass graves were discovered of more than 440 bodies buried in a forest northeast of the city. Among the dead, some had reportedly died as a result of shelling and airstrikes. Forensic investigations and questioning of witnesses were ongoing as of 16 September 2022.[^cite_note-213]&#x20;

## Kharkiv Oblast

See also: [Russian occupation of Kharkiv Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_occupation_of_Kharkiv_Oblast)

### Bombardment of Kharkiv

Main article: [Kharkiv strikes (2022–present)](74401306.md)

See also: [February 2022 Kharkiv cluster bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/February_2022_Kharkiv_cluster_bombing), [March 2022 Kharkiv cluster bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_2022_Kharkiv_cluster_bombing), [April 2022 Kharkiv cluster bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April_2022_Kharkiv_cluster_bombing), and [Kharkiv dormitories missile strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kharkiv_dormitories_missile_strike)



Residential buildings in [Kharkiv Oblast](494901.md) shelled by Russian forces

During and after the [Battle of Kharkiv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Kharkiv_\(2022\)), extensive parts of residential areas have been destroyed by Russian shelling.&#x20;

According to an HRW report published on 4 March, on 28 February, at around 10\:00 AM, Russian forces fired cluster munitions with [Grad rockets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BM-21_Grad) into at least three different residential areas in [Kharkiv](2788558.md),[^cite_note-Kharkiv-214] killing at least nine civilians and injuring another 37.[^cite_note-Kharkiv-214] The city's mayor, [Ihor Terekhov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ihor_Terekhov), said that four people were killed when they left a shelter to get water, and a family of two parents and three children were burned alive in their car.[^cite_note-215] The locations hit were residential buildings and a playground,[^cite_note-WP_2802-216] dispersed between the [Industrialnyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrialnyi_District,_Kharkiv) and [Shevchenkivskyi Districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shevchenkivskyi_District,_Kharkiv). Explosions in the city were recorded as late as 2\:23 PM.[^cite_note-Kharkiv-214]&#x20;

On 1 March, a shell damaged a boarding school for blind children.[^cite_note-HRW_Kharkiv-217] As of 4 March, 122 civilians, including five children, had been killed in the Kharkiv Oblast, according to the Kharkiv Regional Police.[^cite_note-218] Out of an initial population of 1.8 million, only 500,000 people remained in Kharkiv by 7 March.[^cite_note-HRW_Kharkiv-217] On 18 March, the number of civilians reportedly killed in Kharkiv exceeded 450 as a consequence of the use of cluster munitions and explosive weapons in heavily populated areas of the city.[^cite_note-HRW_Deadly_Attacks-219] On 24 March, a cluster munition attack killed eight people, and fifteen were injured while queuing for humanitarian aid together with hundreds of civilians near the Akademika Pavlova metro station.[^cite_note-AI_Kharkiv-220][^cite_note-Report_OHCHR-17]<sup>: para. 27-30 </sup> On 24 March 2022, a Russian missile strike hit a shopping mall parking lot near the [Akademika Pavlova metro station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akademika_Pavlova_\(Kharkiv_Metro\)).[^cite_note-Amnesty_June_2022-221] At the time, hundreds of people were waiting outside a post office in the mall to obtain [humanitarian aid](434679.md).[^cite_note-222] Six people were killed and at least 15 further were injured.[^cite_note-223] Two further cluster bombings damaged the nearby Holy Trinity Church, where volunteers were preparing humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-Amnesty_June_2022-221]&#x20;



Residential building in the center of Kharkiv destroyed by a Russian missile strike



Aftermath of the [Kharkiv dormitories missile strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kharkiv_dormitories_missile_strike), 17 August 2022

On 15 April 2022 in the afternoon, during the battle of Kharkiv, the Russian army fired 9N210/9N235 cluster bombs into the Industrialnyi District, striking a residential area and a playground on Myru Street.[^cite_note-Amnesty_June_2022-221][^cite_note-BBC_News_12_June_2022-224] Nine civilians died and 35 were injured, including children. The local hospital received wounded people with pieces of steel rod and shrapnel in their limbs.[^cite_note-Amnesty_June_2022-221] Overall, the cluster bombs detonated over an area of 700 square metres.[^cite_note-BBC_News_12_June_2022-224]&#x20;

Human Rights Watch investigated the attack and concluded that the Russian forces used [Smerch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BM-30_Smerch) cluster munition rockets, which disperse dozens of submunitions or bomblets in the air.[^cite_note-Kharkiv-214] As there were no military targets within 400 metres (1,300 ft) of these strikes and due to the indiscriminate use of these weapons against densely populated areas, HRW described these strikes as possible war crimes.[^cite_note-Kharkiv-214] On 13 May, CNN reported that newly collected evidence identified Colonel General [Alexander Zhuravlyov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Zhuravlyov) commanding the 79th Rocket Artillery Brigade, ordered the use 17 cluster bombs, the 300mm Smerch Cluster Rocket, to be used against civilian targets in Kharkiv on 27–28 February.[^cite_note-225]&#x20;

On 13 June Amnesty International published a report on what it called the "relentless campaign of indiscriminate bombardments against Kharkiv" causing "wholesale destruction" in the city from 24 February until late April.[^cite_note-AI_Kharkiv-220] The human rights organisation's researchers found fragments of seven [cluster munition](79745.md) strikes in different neighbourhoods of Kharkiv and gathered evidence of the use of scatterable [land mines](18172.md) and [Grad rockets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BM-21_Grad). Amnesty International documented a total of 28 [indiscriminate strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiscriminate_attack) in populated areas of Kharkiv which they claim may constitute war crimes and which caused hundreds of civilian casualties and injured many more.[^cite_note-AI_Kharkiv-220]&#x20;

On 11 July 2022, a Russian wave of shelling killed six people and injured 31 in the Ukrainian city of Kharkiv.[^cite_note-AFPkharkiv-192] The mayor, Ihor Terekhov, said that the areas shelled were [residential areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Residential_area) with "no military significance," including several civilian houses, stores, a tire repair store, and a school.[^cite_note-226][^cite_note-227] [Reuters](18998750.md) confirmed that at least one residential structure had been hit.[^cite_note-swissinfo-228][^cite_note-229] Authorities said that six civilians had died, including a 17-year-old teenager and his father.[^cite_note-AFPkharkiv-192] [Oleh Synyehubov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleh_Synyehubov) said that the Russians had used "[artillery](2508.md), [multiple rocket launchers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiple_rocket_launcher), and tank attacks" on Kharkiv.[^cite_note-swissinfo-228]&#x20;

On 17 August 2022, at 4\:30 am, several rockets fired from Belgorod hit the [Slobidskyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slobidskyi_District) and [Saltivskyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saltivskyi_District) districts of Kharkiv.[^cite_note-bbcRUkharkiv-230] In the Slobidskyi District, a four-story hostel of a tram depot was hit together with an adjacent repair workshop and a neighboring non-residential building.[^cite_note-231] The second missile attack was carried out at 21\:30 and destroyed a three-story hostel in the Saltivskyi District, where people with hearing impairments lived.[^cite_note-bbcRUkharkiv-230] The missile attack caused a fire, and the building was completely destroyed.[^cite_note-232][^cite_note-233] In the Slobidskyi District, two people were initially killed, with the bodies of six people subsequently being excavated from under the ruins; 18 were also injured, including two children.[^cite_note-bbcRUkharkiv-230][^cite_note-il-234] Ten units of fire and rescue equipment worked at the scene of the shelling along with forty rescuers of the State Emergency Service.[^cite_note-il-234] In the Saltivskyi district, at least 19 people were killed and 22 injured,[^cite_note-235] including an 11-year-old child,[^cite_note-bbcRUkharkiv-230][^cite_note-236] in total, 27 (including 1 child) were killed,[^cite_note-237] and 44 (including 3 children) were injured.[^cite_note-bbcRUkharkiv-230] On 19 August, mourning was declared in Kharkiv.[^cite_note-il-234]&#x20;

The [Russian Defense Ministry](7875884.md) confirmed the missile attack on Kharkiv in its briefing. According to their version, "a high-precision ground-based weapon hit a temporary base for foreign mercenaries," and as a result, "more than 90 militants were destroyed."[^cite_note-238] [President of Ukraine](20688479.md) [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) said, "When you hear about Kharkiv [Saltivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saltivka), it's pain again. Pain for all Ukraine. Pain for Kharkiv," he wrote. "Rocket attack... On the hostel... The building is completely destroyed." The president described the killing of residents as "a vile and cynical blow to civilians, which has no justification and demonstrates the impotence of the aggressor."[^cite_note-bbc-239] According to the head of the military administration of the Kharkiv Oblast, Oleh Synyehubov, "The Russians brutally and purposefully attacked civilians. And now in their so-called "media," they are spreading another fake about "military facilities." There are no military installations. Exclusively civilian facilities, including pensioners and children. This is real terrorism, which only fiends are capable of!"[^cite_note-rus-240]&#x20;

On 6 April 2024, Ukrainian authorities said that Russian missile attacks had killed seven people and wounded a dozen in Kharkiv, with the strikes damaging administrative buildings, dormitories, shops, high-rise buildings, and a kindergarten. The Kharkiv police said that Russia attacked the city with two S-300 missiles and then engaged with drones during the rescue operations.[^cite_note-k518-241]&#x20;



Damage from the 30 August 2024 glide bomb attack on Kharkiv, which killed six people

On 30 August 2024, at least five people, including a 14-year-old girl, were killed in Kharkiv by a reported Russian [glide bomb](233100.md) attack that struck a 12-story apartment building and the playground in front of it. Dozens of people were also wounded in the attack.[^cite_note-h686-242]&#x20;

On 28 October 2024, a Russian guided bomb attack on Kharkiv shattered much of the [Derzhprom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Derzhprom), a building placed on the "tentative" list of [UNESCO World Heritage sites](44940.md). Six people were injured in the attack, with another 13 being wounded in an earlier overnight bomb attack.[^cite_note-z950-81]&#x20;

On 31 October 2024, three people were killed, two of them children, and 35 people were injured in a Russian attack on an apartment block in Kharkiv. According to [Oleh Syniehubov](68861798.md), Kharkiv's governor, Russia used a highly explosive [FAB-500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FAB-500) bomb to strike the block.[^cite_note-a391-243]&#x20;

On 29 April 2025, regional officials said that dozens of people had been injured after Russian drone strikes on Kharkiv, including two children and a pregnant woman.[^cite_note-s456-60][^cite_note-q993-59]&#x20;

On 2 May 2025, Russia launched a mass drone strike on Kharkiv, striking 12 locations across four of the city's central districts, with officials saying that the attack wounded 46 people and damaged civilian infrastructure, including a high-rise apartment block.[^cite_note-v357-244][^cite_note-e346-245]&#x20;

On 10–11 June 2025, Russian drone strikes killed three people and injured 60, nine children aged between two and 15 being among the injured, caused by 17 drones striking two residential areas, according to officials.[^cite_note-g035-246]&#x20;

On 24 July 2025, regional officials said that two people were killed and at least 33 wounded when Russian glide bombs struck a residential neighbourhood. Regional governor Syniehubov said a month-old infant and a 10-year-old child were among the wounded.[^cite_note-s774-247]&#x20;

On 28 July 2025, a Russian strike on a queue for humanitarian aid in the north-eastern Kharkiv region killed five people.[^cite_note-k906-248]&#x20;



Destruction from the Russian drone strike on Kharkiv, 18 August 2025, which killed a whole family

On 18 August 2025, there were multiple Russian strikes on Kharkiv. The local mayor said that a ballistic missile had wounded 11 people, while hours later a Russian drone attack on a residential building killed seven people, an entire family including a toddler and her brother aged 16 being among the killed. Authorities also said that 23 people had been wounded in the drone attack, which reduced part of the building to rubble and caused fires across multiple floors. The attacks took place hours before Zelenskyy and several European officials were expected to have talks with U.S. President Trump, with Zelenskyy denouncing the attacks as "Moscow trying to pressure Ukraine and Europe" and "humiliate diplomatic efforts to end the war."[^cite_note-r821-249][^cite_note-o373-250]&#x20;

On 22 October 2025, officials said that Russia had struck a kindergarten in Kharkiv with children in attendance around 9 AM local time, setting it ablaze and killing one person while wounding seven others, six of them children. The attack caused the evacuation of dozens of children, reportedly causing [acute stress reactions](1585862.md) in many of them. The attack took place hours after Trump-Putin talks collapsed.[^cite_note-e995-251][^cite_note-t015-252]&#x20;

On 17 November 2025, a Russian missile strike on Kharkiv damaged residential blocks in the city center, killing three people and wounding 11 according to regional officials, with four minors being among the injured.[^cite_note-p072-253][^cite_note-m887-254]&#x20;

### Kupiansk civilian convoy massacre

Main article: [Kupiansk civilian convoy shelling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kupiansk_civilian_convoy_shelling)

### Hroza missile attack



Bodies of Ukrainians killed in the Hroza missile attack

Main article: [Hroza missile attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hroza_missile_attack)

On 5 October 2023, Russian forces attacked the small village of [Hroza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hroza,_Kharkiv_Oblast) with an [Iskander missile](5607520.md), killing 59[^cite_note-255] people and wounding many more. It targeted a café that was hosting a memorial gathering for a local Ukrainian soldier. The strike killed a fifth of the village's inhabitants, including the fallen soldier's widow and son,[^cite_note-256] and is one of the deadliest attacks on civilians during the war.[^cite_note-257] Ukrainian authorities said the attack was clearly deliberate, and there was speculation that a spy gave Russian forces the location.[^cite_note-258] [Vasily Nebenzya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vasily_Nebenzya), Russia's Permanent Representative to the UN, claimed that many of those attending the memorial were "Nazis."[^cite_note-259]&#x20;

### Volnovakha massacre

Main article: [Volnovakha massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volnovakha_massacre)

### November 2025 Kruhliakivka drone attack

On 4 November 2025, a pre-trial war-crimes investigation in Kharkiv was opened after a video recorded near [Kruhliakivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kruhliakivka) appeared to show a Russian drone killing two civilians and their dog. According to the statement by Kharkiv's Regional Prosecutor's Office, the civilians were carrying a [white flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_flag) as they were walking along the road when they were struck and killed by the drone.[^cite_note-u346-260][^cite_note-s710-261]&#x20;

### November 2025 Berestyn missile attack

On 18 November 2025, regional officials said that a 17-year-old girl had been killed and nine others wounded in an overnight attack by Russian missiles on [Berestyn](6863263.md).[^cite_note-c826-262][^cite_note-y082-263][^cite_note-b813-264]&#x20;

### January 2026 missile and drone strikes

On 2 January 2026, officials said that two people, preliminary information suggesting a mother and her three-year-old child, had been killed and around 28 people wounded after a Russian missile almost completely destroyed a residential apartment block.[^cite_note-x416-265]&#x20;

On 13 January, officials said four people had been killed and six wounded in a Russian drone attack, which took place hours after the US had accused Russia of "dangerous and inexplicable escalation" of the war.[^cite_note-a178-266]&#x20;

On 27 January, the [Kharkiv Human Rights Protection Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kharkiv_Human_Rights_Protection_Group) said that a passenger train had been struck by Russian drones while travelling in Kharkiv Oblast, with one drone directly hitting a carriage and engulfing it in flames. At least five people were reported to have been killed in the attack, which Zelenskyy described as an "act of terrorism."[^cite_note-s631-267][^cite_note-t471-268]&#x20;

### March 2026 missile and drone strikes

On 7 March 2026, regional officials said that ten people, including two children, had been killed after a Russian ballistic missile hit a five-story residential building; 15 people were also reported to have been injured, including three children.[^cite_note-d726-269][^cite_note-g474-270] On 11 March, officials said that a Russian drone striking a civilian business resulted in two people being killed and five seriously injured.[^cite_note-v589-271]&#x20;

### May 2026 missile strike

On 4 May 2026, officials said that a Russian missile strike in the morning on [Merefa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merefa) killed seven people and wounded at least 36, including a two-year-old boy. Governor Syniehubov said that infrastructure was also damaged, including 10 houses and multiple shops, with Reuters footage showing a crater on the road. Regional prosecutors said that Russia appeared to have used an Iskander type of ballistic missile for the attack.[^cite_note-g473-272]&#x20;

### June 2026 missile and drone strikes

On 9 June 2026, a nighttime missile attack on [Chuhuiv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuhuiv) killed three people, including a 22-year old pregnant woman, also injuring six people and damaging civilian infrastructure according to regional prosecutors. In the city of Kharkiv, governor Syniehubov said that Russian drone attacks resulted 16 people seeking medical assistance.[^cite_note-b834-273]&#x20;

### July 2026 attacks

On 3 July 2026, regional head Syniehubov said that a Russian drone strike on a residential building in [Lozova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lozova) resulted in six people being injured, including three children. One of the children, a 10-year-old girl, died the next day in hospital after suffering severe burns.[^cite_note-e297-274][^cite_note-n301-275]&#x20;

On 17 July, Syniehubov said that Russian attacks had killed one person in [Vysokyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vysokyi), while injuring 14 people across the region.[^cite_note-g420-40]&#x20;

### August 2026 village attacks

On 18 August 2026, officials said a Russian ballistic missile strike killed ten civilians and wounded eight more in [Pechenihy](55156003.md), completely destroying several homes.[^cite_note-276][^cite_note-277]&#x20;

### Chuhuiv strike

On 5 September 2026, officials said that a Russian drone struck a residential house in Chuhuiv, killing three members of the same family, including an 11-year-old boy. A woman was also seriously wounded.[^cite_note-j276-278]&#x20;

## Kherson Oblast

### Shelling of Kherson

Main article: [Kherson strikes (2022–present)](73852715.md)



The dead in an [ATB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ATB-Market) store on 3 May 2023

The city of [Kherson](1025861.md) was [liberated from Russian occupation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_of_Kherson) in November 2022. Russian troops continued to shell the city from the other side of the [Dnipro River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dnipro_River). On 3 May 2023, Russian artillery [struck a supermarket, a train station, and residential buildings in Kherson](73852715.md), killing 23 people and wounding 46. Among those killed were three engineers repairing damage inflicted on the power grid in earlier Russian strikes.[^cite_note-279]&#x20;

On 3 December 2025, the rector of the [Kherson National Technical University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kherson_National_Technical_University) reported that a scientific staff member of the university, Tetiana Asauluk, had been killed by Russian shelling.[^cite_note-g323-280]&#x20;

### Deliberate drone attacks on Kherson civilians

Main article: [Human safari (terror campaign)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_safari_\(terror_campaign\))

In October 2024, Forbes reported that Russians were using drones to deliberately target civilians in Kherson and then "proudly" sharing the footage of the attacks on social media. Attacks being "shared and celebrated" including strikes on commuters, people at bus stops, and children playing in parks. This terror campaign has become known as the "Human Safari." One of the instances described as a "typical case" was a Ukrainian mother-of-two who was cycling home when a Russian drone spotted her and followed her until dropping a grenade, which injured her with shrapnel, leaving her unable to walk. Imagery of the attack was posted on a Russian Telegram channel with a winking face emoji and falsely describing her as a soldier.[^cite_note-p249-281][^cite_note-o093-282]&#x20;

In July 2025, local authorities said a one-year-old boy who was staying with his great-grandmother in [Pravdyne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pravdyne) had been struck by a Russian drone. The child died on the scene from injuries sustained from the explosion, and his grandmother was injured. The attack was part of a wider attack on Kherson, which wounded 17 people across the region.[^cite_note-o677-283][^cite_note-h290-284]&#x20;

Other than targeting individual civilians, Russian drones using a napalm-like mixture to start fires burning down entire neighbourhoods and scattering mines powerful enough to destroy a tire or blow a foot off have also been noted.[^cite_note-p249-281]&#x20;

### Gammalsvenskby white phosphorus attacks
