The former village of [Gammalsvenskby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gammalsvenskby) within [Zmiivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zmiivka) has been the target of several Russian attacks making use of [white phosphorus munitions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_phosphorus_munitions) to deliberately target civil [infrastructure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infrastructure)[^cite_note-285] to cause as much devastation to the local civilian population as possible and have led to several civilian deaths. These attacks constitute direct [terror bombings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terror_Bombing) directly targeted at civilians,[^cite_note-286] including the deliberate bombing of the local school, resulting in the death of one employee and leaving another severely wounded.[^cite_note-SVT_Nyheter_2023_w908-287]&#x20;

### 2025 Christmas attack

On 26 December 2025, senators from the US Democratic and Republican parties condemned Russian attacks on Ukrainian civilians gathering to celebrate Christmas, with three people reported to be injured by shelling in central Kherson.[^cite_note-x065-38]&#x20;

### January 2026 shelling

According to Ukrainian officials, Russian shelling struck a bus in central Kherson on 30 January 2026, killing the driver and wounding five other civilians.[^cite_note-p478-288][^cite_note-f970-289]&#x20;

### May 2026 playground strike

On 27 May 2026, local authorities said that Russian forces launched a [multiple rocket launcher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiple_rocket_launcher) attack on Kherson, which struck a playground in the city and killed a father present with his two daughters ages three and six, who were both injured, the mother of the children as well as a 50-year-old man were also reported injured.[^cite_note-r215-290][^cite_note-f785-291]&#x20;

### July 2026 attacks

On 17 July 2026, the Kherson military administration said that Russia had attacked 38 settlements, including the regional center, resulting in eight people being injured.[^cite_note-g420-40]&#x20;

## Kyiv Oblast

See also: [Kyiv offensive (2022)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyiv_offensive_\(2022\)) and [Kyiv strikes (2022–present)](73869112.md)

### Kyiv missile and drone strikes

See also: [Battle of Kyiv (2022)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Kyiv_\(2022\))



Residential building in Kyiv after being hit by a missile, 26 February 2022

Ukraine's capital, [Kyiv](585629.md), a city of some three million people, was among the first targets of Russian airstrikes.[^cite_note-292] Kindergartens and orphanages were also shelled.[^cite_note-293]&#x20;



Dead civilian in Kyiv during the 10 October missile attack

On 10 October 2022, at 8\:00 a.m. [local time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_in_Ukraine), several explosions rang out in the [Shevchenkivskyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shevchenkivskyi_District,_Kyiv) and [Solomianskyi District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solomianskyi_District) of Kyiv.[^cite_note-294] This was announced by the mayor of the capital, [Vitali Klitschko](555483.md).[^cite_note-295] According to [Anton Herashchenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anton_Herashchenko), the adviser to the head of the Ministry of Internal Affairs, one of the rockets in Kyiv fell near the monument to [Mykhailo Hrushevsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mykhailo_Hrushevsky) on Volodymyrskaya street.[^cite_note-296] A missile struck the [Kyiv Glass Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bridge_over_Saint_Volodymyr_Descent) at 8\:18 local time.[^cite_note-297] The blast wave damaged the building and the roof of the central station Kyiv-Passenger.[^cite_note-dwkyiv-298] The Russian Armed Forces damaged Ukrainian cultural and educational buildings, including the [Taras Shevchenko National University of Kyiv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taras_Shevchenko_National_University_of_Kyiv), the [Khanenko Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khanenko_Museum), and the [Taras Shevchenko National Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Museum_Taras_Shevchenko).[^cite_note-299]&#x20;

According to Rostyslav Smirnov, adviser to the Minister of Internal Affairs of Ukraine, at least eight people were killed and 24 were injured as a result of several hits in different places in Kyiv.[^cite_note-dwkyiv-298] Areas struck by missiles included nearby a children's playground.[^cite_note-300] A fire broke out in six cars, and more than 15 cars were damaged.[^cite_note-nv.ua-301] The [Kyiv Metro's red line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyiv_Metro#Line_M1_\(red_line\)) and the [Teatralna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teatralna_\(Kyiv_Metro\))–[Golden Gate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoloti_Vorota_\(Kyiv_Metro\)) interchange node subway trains stopped running, and the underground tunnels became shelters for citizens.[^cite_note-302][^cite_note-bbc-63197747-303] Smoke also rose over the [CHP-6](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=CHP-6\&action=edit\&redlink=1).[^cite_note-svoboda-32072927-304]&#x20;

#### March 2025 drone attack

On 22 March 2025, Ukrainian officials said that a large-scale Russian drone attack on Kyiv killed three people, including a five-year-old child and her father, and injured eight people. According to the [Ukrainian Air Force](1135769.md), Russia had launched 147 drones targeting several parts of the country. Debris from destroyed drones caused damage throughout the city, causing fires in high-rise apartment buildings. The attack happened less than a week after Russia announced a "partial 30-day ceasefire" amid a US push for a peace deal.[^cite_note-l340-305][^cite_note-y790-306]&#x20;

#### April 2025 joint missile and drone barrage

Main article: [April 2025 Russian attack on Kyiv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April_2025_Russian_attack_on_Kyiv)



Destroyed apartment building in Kyiv, 24 April 2025. The Russian airstrikes killed 12 people.

On 24 April 2025, Russia struck Kyiv for an hour with both missiles and drones, killing at least 12 people and wounding approximately 90 people. The strikes began around 1 AM and hit at least five neighborhoods, causing major damage to residential buildings. The [Associated Press](18935732.md) described the attack as the deadliest attack on Kyiv since July, and it drew criticism of Putin by US President Trump, who the AP described as "reluctant to criticize the Kremlin." Following the attack, also carried out amid US-led peace efforts, Trump said the attack was "bad timing" and told Putin to "stop."[^cite_note-b044-307][^cite_note-p470-308][^cite_note-z248-309]&#x20;

#### May 2025 air attack

In May 2025, Russia launched a three-day-long wave of drone and missile attacks on Ukraine, with the primary target being Kyiv. The attack started on 24 May and involved hundreds of drones and dozens of missiles and was described by Reuters as the war's largest air attack on Ukraine up until that point. According to officials, no deaths were reported in Kyiv, but at least 15 people were injured in the city, and apartment buildings were damaged. At least 12 people were killed in the attack, including four people in the region around Kyiv and three children in [Zhytomyr](1025103.md). The attack started hours after Russia and Ukraine began a significant prisoner exchange, seen as a first step to a ceasefire between the countries.[^cite_note-j101-310][^cite_note-g348-311][^cite_note-t877-312][^cite_note-h782-313]&#x20;

#### June 2025 strikes



Destroyed apartment building in Kyiv on 17 June 2025. The Russian airstrike killed 28 people.

On 10 June 2025, officials said that Russia launched one of its largest strikes on Kyiv, causing widespread damage in seven out of ten of the city's districts and injuring four people.[^cite_note-d385-314] A week later, Russia again launched a sustained missile and drone bombardment on the city early on 17 June; the attack caused the demolishing of a nine-story apartment building, killing 28 people and injuring over 100.[^cite_note-n144-315][^cite_note-k422-316][^cite_note-j153-317]&#x20;

#### July 2025 strikes

On 10 July 2025, Interior Minister [Ihor Klymenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ihor_Klymenko) confirmed that two women had been killed in Kyiv following Russian drone strikes hitting eight districts in the city. The mayor, Klitschko, said that a primary healthcare center in the [Podilskyi District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Podilskyi_District) was almost completely destroyed in the attack.[^cite_note-z269-318]&#x20;

On 31 July, at least 28 people were killed by a barrage of Russian missiles and drones striking several districts across Kyiv. Ukrainian officials said that three children, the youngest three years old, were among the dead. 159 people were also reported to have been wounded in the attack, which collapsed an apartment block. Several other buildings were damaged, including a hospital ward for children in Shevchenkivskyi, which was damaged by a shockwave.[^cite_note-k708-319]&#x20;

#### August 2025 strikes on civilians, EU mission and British Council



Destroyed apartment building after Russian airstrikes on 28 August 2025, which killed 25 people

On 28 August 2025, the Russian Armed Forces launched nearly 600 drones and more than 30 [ballistic](38025.md) and [cruise missiles](6590.md) at [Kyiv](585629.md), Ukraine, striking multiple residential areas, killing 23 people, including four children, and wounding dozens more. A five-story residential building was destroyed, and the [EU](9317.md) mission and nearby [British Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Council) were damaged. Officials said three of the children killed were aged two, 14, and 17. Several other youngsters were wounded.[^cite_note-320]&#x20;

#### October 2025 drone and missile attacks

On 10 October 2025, Ukrainian authorities said that Russia targeted civilian and energy infrastructure in Kyiv with drones and missiles, wounding 20 people, damaging residential buildings, and causing blackouts.[^cite_note-r538-321][^cite_note-t878-322][^cite_note-d067-323]&#x20;

On 26 October, three people were killed and 29 wounded after Russian drones struck residential buildings in the city. State Emergency Services said that a 19-year-old woman along with her mother were among the dead, while seven children were among the injured, with victims having to be rescued out of the upper floors of a nine-story apartment block after a fire broke out in the building.[^cite_note-f159-324]&#x20;

#### November 2025 attack

In November 2025, state emergency services said that two people had been killed and 38 wounded in a Russian overnight attack, which also cut power to half the city, leaving at least 500,000 people without electricity.[^cite_note-q920-325][^cite_note-k559-326]&#x20;

#### January 2026 strikes

On 5 January 2026, authorities said Russia had struck a private medical facility in Kyiv, killing one patient and wounding three others, as well as causing night-time evacuations in freezing temperatures. The same air attack also killed a man in his 70s in [Fastiv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fastiv).[^cite_note-o600-327][^cite_note-u350-328][^cite_note-t757-329]&#x20;

On 8 January, four people were killed and 25 injured in Kyiv as Russia launched a massive overnight strike across the country, including a [oreshnik](78416645.md) ballistic missile, which, according to Ukrainian authorities, had struck [Lviv](49968.md).[^cite_note-e423-330]&#x20;

#### April 2026 strikes

On 3 April 2026, [Mykola Kalashnyk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mykola_Kalashnyk), the head of the regional military administration, said that one person had been killed and at least eight wounded in Russian strikes on [Bucha](3310001.md), Fastiv, and [Obukhiv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obukhiv). A veterinary clinic close to Kyiv was also struck, killing around 20 animals.[^cite_note-l863-331] On 16 April, Klitschko said that a Russian drone and missile attack resulted in the deaths of two people in Kyiv, a 12-year-old boy and a 35-year-old woman, with at least 10 people being wounded.[^cite_note-p562-332]&#x20;

#### May 2026 strikes

Russia launched heavy attacks on Ukraine in May 2026. On 14 May, an apartment block was destroyed in Kyiv during heavy Russian bombardment that had been going on since the day before, resulting in the deaths of 24 people, including three children.[^cite_note-333][^cite_note-f053-334][^cite_note-i653-335]&#x20;

On 24 May, Ukrainian officials described a massive Russian attack on Kyiv and the surrounding region, including the use of a ″oreshnik″ hypersonic ballistic missile, which Zelenskyy said hit the city of [Bila Tserkva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bila_Tserkva). Officials reported that the attack resulted in four deaths and around 100 injuries, and Kyiv mayor Klitschko said that damage had been recorded in every district of the city, including starting a fire in a school.[^cite_note-t275-336]&#x20;

#### June 2026 strikes

On 2 June 2026, a large-scale attack of drones and cruise missiles killed 22 (6 in Kyiv & 16 in Dnipro) and injured over 100 according to Ukrainian authorities. More than 600 drones and dozens of missiles hit key civilian infrastructure, including a medical facility and residential buildings, and damaged a kindergarten.[^cite_note-cnn_kyiv_june2026-337][^cite_note-reuters_smoke_kyiv-338]&#x20;

##### 14 June bombardment and damage of UNESCO monastery

On 14, local authorities said that a large Russian attack on Kyiv using drones and missiles resulted in the death of at least five people while injuring more than 30 people. During the attack, the 11th century [UNESCO](21786641.md)-listed [Kyiv Pechersk Lavra](478837.md), which in 2023 was added to the World Heritage in Danger list due to ″the threat of destruction the Russian offensive poses″, was engulfed in flames. Kyiv mayor Klitschko said that around 140,000 households were also left without electricity in the northern part of the city as a result of the attack.[^cite_note-v224-339]&#x20;

#### July 2026 large-scale attacks on Kyiv

On 2 July 2026, several media outlets reported on a massive attack on Kyiv by Russia, with *CNN* saying that Russia targeted residential buildings with weapons including ballistic missiles, jet-powered drones and loitering munitions, killing at least 30 people. Timur Tkachenko, head of Kyiv's military administration, also said 91 people were injured. As a result of the attack, Kyiv's metro authorities said that 52,500 people, including 4,500 children, spent the night sheltering in underground stations.[^cite_note-a162-340][^cite_note-i855-341][^cite_note-f056-342]&#x20;

Days later, Russia launched another missile and drone attack on the city and surrounding region, killing at least 27 people, including an entire family that were pulled out of the rubble of a residential building, while injuring dozens more according to officials. According to Ukraine's Air Force, none of the ballistic missiles Russia launched were shot down by air defense, which Zelenskyy said was because of an ″insufficient supply of interceptor missiles″.[^cite_note-l101-343]&#x20;

On 30 July, local authorities said the city was targeted with ballistic missiles, killing one person and wounding two, while the metro reported more than 56,000 people sheltering in subway stations overnight.[^cite_note-r672-344]&#x20;

#### August 2026 continued ballistic missile strikes

On 1 August 2026, Zelenskyy said that at least nine people were killed and dozens injured in a Russian ballistic missile attack. According to the emergency services, two children were among the injured in the [Darnytskyi District](1939698.md). The foreign ministry of [Lithuania](17675.md) also said that a missile landed near the Lithuanian embassy, causing damage and scattering debris on its compound.[^cite_note-x345-345]&#x20;

On 4 August, Ukrainian officials said that at least 17 people had been killed and dozens injured in another Russian ballistic missile attack on Kyiv and its suburbs, with Zelenskyy again highlighting Ukraine's lack of interceptor missiles to defend against Russian attacks.[^cite_note-y823-346]&#x20;

On 21 August, officials said that 16 people had been killed and 33 injured in what *The Guardian* described as ″one of the most intense aerial attacks″ on Kyiv. Mayor Klitschko said that damaged buildings included a children's medical centre, a school, and homes, while the Ukrainian Air Force said Russia used various hypersonic and ballistic missiles, including North Korean [KN-23 ballistic missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hwasong-11C) and drones.[^cite_note-e825-347]&#x20;

On 29 August, Ukrainian officials said a Russian drone strike on a warehouse in the village Myla killed at least 37 people and injured over 40. The resulting explosions impacted residential buildings and a care home, and Zelenskyy announced that a criminal investigation into official negligence was underway as ammunition may have been stored in the warehouse, which would be against Ukraine's regulations.[^cite_note-x789-348]&#x20;

#### September 2026 drone swarms as Russian battlefield progress slows

On 1 September 2026, authorities reported that Russian attacks, ongoing for the sixth straight day, had killed 12 people and wounded ″many more″ in Kyiv and the surrounding region. *Reuters* said Russia had escalated its air attacks recently amidst slowing progress on the battlefield, sending swarms of drones into the region day and night.[^cite_note-d109-208]&#x20;

On 9 September, Kyiv authorities said that a woman had been killed and nine people wounded as Russian drones repeatedly targeted the city during the day, striking several residential buildings.[^cite_note-d108-349]&#x20;

On 20 September, regional authorities said that four people had been killed, including a mother and her two children who were three-years old, while another child was killed in a separate drone strike. One person was also reported injured.[^cite_note-d109-208]&#x20;

On 23 September, Kyiv authorities reported that two people were killed and 22 injured in further Russian drone attacks, with one drone striking near the [Kyiv National University of Construction and Architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyiv_National_University_of_Construction_and_Architecture), though no students or staff were reported to have been hurt.[^cite_note-u160-350]&#x20;

On 30 September, the *[Kyiv Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyiv_Post)* reported that three people had been killed, including a child, and eight wounded in continued Russian drone attacks on the city.[^cite_note-l732-351]&#x20;

#### October 2026 drone strike on school

On 1 October 2026, Mayor Klitschko said a Russian drone had struck a school in the Solomianskyi district. At the time of the strike, the children and teachers in the school were in a shelter, avoiding casualties. Kyiv authorities further reported drone attacks on the same day resulting in fires breaking out in an apartment building and a warehouse, resulting in one person being injured.[^cite_note-m236-352]&#x20;

### Bombing of Borodianka



Destroyed residential buildings in Borodianka, March 2022

Main article: [Bombing of Borodianka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombing_of_Borodianka)

As Russian forces fought in and near Kyiv, [Borodianka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borodianka), which is on a strategically important road,[^cite_note-353] was targeted by numerous Russian airstrikes.[^cite_note-Europe1-354] Most of the buildings in the town were destroyed,[^cite_note-reuters-razed-355] including almost all of its main street.[^cite_note-npr-return-356] Russian bombs struck the centers of buildings and caused them to collapse while the frames remained standing.[^cite_note-reuters-razed-355][^cite_note-npr-return-356] Many civilians were also reportedly killed by cluster munitions during the attacks.[^cite_note-TheGuardianBucha-357] [Oleksiy Reznikov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleksii_Reznikov), [minister of defense](14284651.md), said many residents were [buried alive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buried_alive) by airstrikes and lay dying for up to a week.[^cite_note-Civilians_buried_alive-358] Some residents hid in caves for 38 days.[^cite_note-decodeurs-359]&#x20;

Only a few hundred residents remained in Borodianka by the time the Russians withdrew, with roughly 90% of residents having fled,[^cite_note-npr-return-356] and an unknown number dead in the rubble.[^cite_note-Europe1-354] Borodianka's mayor estimated at least 200 dead.[^cite_note-360] [Agence France-Presse](471981.md) arrived in Borodianka on 5 April. The AFP did not see any bodies but reported widespread destruction and that some homes "simply no longer existed." The human death toll remained unclear: one resident reported that he knew of at least five civilians killed, but that others were beneath the rubble and that no one had yet attempted to extricate them.[^cite_note-361][^cite_note-decodeurs-359] According to [Europe 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Europe_1), ten days after the Russian army had left, firefighters were still working to recover bodies from the rubble in order to bury them with dignity. Their work was complicated by the risk of other buildings collapsing. More bodies were discovered daily. Local [morgues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morgue) were overwhelmed, and corpses had to be transported 100 kilometres or more.[^cite_note-Europe1-354]&#x20;

### Irpin refugee column shelling



Civilians fleeing over the Irpin River bridge, March 2022

Main article: [Irpin refugee column shelling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irpin_refugee_column_shelling)

On 6 March 2022, from 9\:30 a.m. until 2 p.m. local time, the Russian Armed Forces repeatedly shelled an [intersection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intersection_\(road\)) in [Irpin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irpin) that hundreds of civilians were using to escape to Kyiv while a Ukrainian artillery position was located nearby. They killed at least eight Ukrainian civilians (including 2 children). Human Rights Watch alleged the Russian army carried out an "unlawful, indiscriminate, and disproportionate attack." The incident was part of an [assault on Irpin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Irpin).&#x20;

On that day, there were hundreds of civilians at the intersection on the P30 road, near the St. George's Ukrainian Orthodox Church, just south of a bridge that the Ukrainian army had destroyed to hinder the Russian invasion. The civilians were fleeing the Russian army's advance from Irpin towards Kyiv. In the intersection near the bridge were a dozen Ukrainian soldiers, some helping the civilians carry their luggage and children.[^cite_note-HRW_March62022-362] The Ukrainian artillery was firing mortar rounds from a position about 180 metres (590 ft) away.[^cite_note-HRW_March62022-362] No agreements had been reached between the parties about a temporary ceasefire or humanitarian corridor.[^cite_note-HRW_March62022-362] Journalists of *[The New York Times](30680.md)* and freelance journalists on the scene report that for several hours the Russian army bombarded the intersection that the civilians were using to flee.[^cite_note-NYT060322-363] The Russians fired explosive projectiles into the area, with projectiles hitting the intersection or the surrounding area every 10 minutes,[^cite_note-HRW_March62022-362] Among the victims were a group of four, including two children, who were killed by a [mortar](324499.md) strike.[^cite_note-364][^cite_note-365]&#x20;

According to [Human Rights Watch](46530.md), it is possible that the projectiles were "observed" by the Russians, who would then know where they were landing and could easily have adjusted the aim away from the intersection. Instead, they engaged in prolonged shelling of the intersection being used by civilians, which indicates "potential recklessness or deliberateness" on their part. The repeated nature of the attacks suggests that Russian forces "violated their obligations under international humanitarian law not to conduct indiscriminate or disproportionate attacks that harm civilians and failed to take all feasible measures to avoid civilian casualties."[^cite_note-HRW_March62022-362] The human rights organisation also stated that the Ukrainian forces "have an obligation to take all feasible precautions to avoid or minimize civilian harm," such as refraining from engaging in combat in populated areas.[^cite_note-HRW_March62022-362]&#x20;

### Bucha massacre

See also: [Battle of Bucha](70246957.md)



Victims of the Bucha massacre

Ukrainian forensic investigations on the [Bucha massacre](70455343.md) revealed that dozens of civilians had been killed by metal darts (["fléchettes"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flechette#Russo-Ukrainian_war)) of a kind used by the Russian army. Bodies from the Bucha-Irpin region showed lesions from small nail-like objects contained in tank or field gun shells. According to witnesses, Russian artillery fired shells that spread fléchettes a few days before retreating from the area at the end of March. While fléchettes are not prohibited under international law, their use in residential areas may qualify as the war crime of [indiscriminate attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiscriminate_attack).[^cite_note-366] The spokesperson for the [Ukrainian Ground Forces](7433327.md) stated that Ukraine's military does not use shells with fléchettes.[^cite_note-367]&#x20;

According to Oleg Tkalenko, deputy chief prosecutor of the Kyiv Oblast, forensic experts found fragments of cluster munitions in bodies from mass graves in Bucha after the retreat of Russian troops. The exact number of civilians killed specifically due to the use of cluster munitions in the village was unknown, but at least 8 out of about 500 of those found were reportedly killed by their use. The Guardian later confirmed the use of RBK-500 cluster munitions with PTAB-submunitions and cluster missiles fired by the BM-30 Smerch in Bucha.[^cite_note-TheGuardianBucha-357] The mayor of the city, [Anatoly Fedoruk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anatoliy_Fedoruk), stated that "Bucha was turned into a [Chechen](6095.md) safari, where land mines were used against civilians." Cluster munition fragments were also found in the nearby city of [Hostomel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hostomel), many of them in animal carcasses.[^cite_note-368]&#x20;

## Khmelnytskyi Oblast

Main article: [Khmelnytskyi strikes (2022–present)](74392967.md)



Destruction in Khmelnytskyi after Russian attack on 22 March 2024

On 9 July 2025, Ukrainian officials said one civilian in [Khmelnytskyi](686060.md) suffered fatal injuries from the falling debris of a Shahed drone. Ukraine's Air Force said that it was part of an aerial assault consisting of 728 drones and 13 missiles, the largest drone attack launched in the war up to that date. It was launched hours after US President Trump pledged more military support for Ukraine and criticized Putin over peace talks.[^cite_note-b675-369][^cite_note-d790-370]&#x20;

## Luhansk Oblast

### Bilohorivka school bombing



Aftermath of the Bilohorivka school bombing

Main article: [Bilohorivka school bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bilohorivka_school_bombing)

On 7 May 2022, Russian forces bombed a school in [Bilohorivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bilohorivka,_Luhansk_Oblast) where about ninety people were seeking shelter from the ongoing fighting during the [Battle of Sievierodonetsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Sievierodonetsk_\(2022\)). The building caught on fire and trapped large numbers of people inside.[^cite_note-371] At least 30 people were rescued.[^cite_note-372] Two people were confirmed to have been killed, but [Governor of Luhansk Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_Luhansk_Oblast) [Serhiy Haidai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serhiy_Haidai) said that the 60 remaining people were believed to have been killed.[^cite_note-373]&#x20;

The attack was condemned by the [Ukrainian Foreign Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_Foreign_Ministry) and [UN Secretary-General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_secretary-general) [Antonio Guterres](471806.md), who said he was "appalled" by the attack. He also reminded that "civilians and civilian infrastructure must always be spared in times of war."[^cite_note-374] [Liz Truss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liz_Truss), the British [foreign secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Secretary_\(United_Kingdom\)), said that she was "horrified" and described the attack as constituting [war crimes](48626.md).[^cite_note-375]&#x20;

### Stara Krasnianka care home attack

Main article: [Stara Krasnianka care house attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stara_Krasnianka_care_house_attack)

On 7 March 2022, the Ukrainian armed forces set up a firing position at a care house in the village of Stara Krasnianka, near [Kreminna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kreminna), [Luhansk Oblast](494904.md), due to the house's strategic location, with the evacuation reportedly impossible due to [mining](18172.md).[^cite_note-Report_OHCHR-17]<sup>: para. 35-36 </sup>[^cite_note-timesofisrael-2022-07-09-376] On 9 March, the Ukrainian forces based at the care house engaged in a first exchange of fire with [Russian-affiliated armed groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_separatist_forces_in_Donbas) without casualties among the civilian residents. On 11 March 2022, pro-Russian separatist forces attacked the care house with heavy weapons while 71 patients with disabilities and 15 members of staff were still inside. A fire broke out and approximately fifty people died. A group of residents fled the house and ran into the forest until they were met five kilometers away by Russian-affiliated armed groups, who provided them with assistance.[^cite_note-Report_OHCHR-17]<sup>: para. 35-36 </sup>[^cite_note-timesofisrael-2022-07-09-376] Ukraine officials accused the Russian forces of deliberately targeting a medical facility and forcefully deporting the survivors.[^cite_note-Ball-2022-377][^cite_note-WoPo_April-378] On 29 June, a report of the [OHCHR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OHCHR), which didn't find "that Russia committed any war crimes." It doesn't say that Ukraine used [human shields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_shield) but "only expressed concern about allegations of this occurring." The report found that Ukrainian forces occupied the building before the attack; thus, care home patients were not the intendend target.[^cite_note-379][^cite_note-timesofisrael-2022-07-09-376]&#x20;

## Bombing of Lviv



Aftermath of the Lviv missile strike, 6 July 2023

Further information: [Lviv strikes (2022–present)](71393746.md)

On 6 July 2023, Russian forces launched ten [Kalibr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalibr_\(missile_family\)) cruise missiles at [Lviv](49968.md), in western Ukraine. One of the guided missiles hit an apartment block in a residential area, killing ten civilians, wounding almost fifty, and causing widespread damage. Most of the other missiles were shot down, but two struck Ukrainian military targets nearby. [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) said the attack should be investigated as a war crime.[^cite_note-380]&#x20;

On 4 September 2024, Russian air attacks struck the [historic centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Town_\(Lviv\)) of Lviv, a [UNESCO World Heritage Site](44940.md), killing civilians and damaging civilian infrastructure and buildings. Lviv mayor [Andryi Sadovyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andriy_Sadovyi) reported that seven people were killed, including four members of the same family, with a mother and her three children being killed in the attack, leaving only the father alive. 66 people required medical assistance, including 10 children. Local authorities said the attack damaged about 50 civilian objects, including homes, medical facilities and architectural landmarks.[^cite_note-m535-381]&#x20;

On 21 August 2025, one person was killed and three were injured by Russian drone and missile strikes that damaged more than 20 civilian buildings, including a nursery and residential homes.[^cite_note-l839-382]&#x20;

On 5 October 2025, officials said that the largest attack on the Lviv region since the beginning of the full-scale invasion had taken place overnight by Russian drones and missiles. According to Lviv's regional head [Maksym Kozytskyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maksym_Kozytskyi), it involved around 163 missiles and drones, targeting civilian infrastructure like residential buildings and hospitals. The attack killed a 15-year-old-girl, as well as three of her family members in the village of Lapaivka, injuring three more people.[^cite_note-v732-383][^cite_note-c124-384]&#x20;

On 24 March 2026, officials said that at least 13 people had been injured in a Russian daytime drone attack on the city, which damaged a residential building close to the 17th century [St. Andrew's Church](13459897.md), a UNESCO World Heritage Site.[^cite_note-p134-385]&#x20;

On 30 July 2026, the Lviv regional military administration said that a cruise missile attack had killed one person and wounded over 30, including children aged six, 13 and 15.[^cite_note-r672-344]&#x20;

## Bombing of Mykolaiv

Main articles: [Mykolaiv strikes (2022–present)](74398144.md) and [Mykolaiv cluster bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mykolaiv_cluster_bombing)



Aftermath of Russian rocket strike on Mykolaiv, 29 June 2022, which killed eight people

Cluster munitions were repeatedly used also on [Mykolaiv](901495.md) during separate attacks on 7, 11 and 13 March 2022, causing civilian casualties and extensive destruction of non-military objects.[^cite_note-HRW_Mykolaiv-386] In the 13 March attack nine civilians, including two children, were killed and 13 injured while waiting in line on the street at a cash machine.[^cite_note-387][^cite_note-Moscow_Times_Mykolaiv-388][^cite_note-Report_OHCHR-17]<sup>: para. 27-30 </sup> The explosions also damaged houses and civilian buildings.[^cite_note-Moscow_Times_Mykolaiv-388] Human Rights Watch analysed the incident and found that the Russian forces used [Smerch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BM-30_Smerch) and [Uragan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BM-27_Uragan) cluster munition on densely populated areas.[^cite_note-HRW_Mykolaiv-386] Due to the inherently indiscriminate nature of cluster munitions, Human Rights Watch described their use in Mykolaiv as a possible Russian war crime.[^cite_note-HRW_Mykolaiv-386]&#x20;

On 28 June 2022, Russian shelling damaged the [Central City Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsentralnyi_Stadion_\(Mykolaiv\)).[^cite_note-389] On the following day, a Russian rocket strike hit a 5-story residential building,[^cite_note-390] killing at least 8 people and injuring 6.[^cite_note-391] On 15 July, the two largest universities of the city were struck by missiles: [Admiral Makarov National University of Shipbuilding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Admiral_Makarov_National_University_of_Shipbuilding) and the Mykolaiv National University.[^cite_note-392] On 29 July, Russian forces bombed a bus stop. 5 were killed and 7 were injured.[^cite_note-393]&#x20;

## Odesa Oblast

### Bombing of Odesa

Main article: [Odesa strikes (2022–present)](70426189.md)



Damage to the [Transfiguration Cathedral in Odesa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transfiguration_Cathedral_in_Odesa) from a Russian missile strike on 23 July 2023



Damage in Odesa from a Russian airstrike on 20 July 2023

On 3 March 2022, the nearby villages of [Zatoka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zatoka,_Odesa_Oblast)[^cite_note-394] and [Bilenke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bilenke,_Odesa_Oblast) were shelled, killing at least one civilian in Bilenke.[^cite_note-395] On 23 April, a Russian missile strike hit two residential buildings.[^cite_note-396] killing eight civilians and wounding 18 or 20, according to Ukraine.[^cite_note-397] One missile that struck a residential building killed a three-month-old baby, the mother, and the baby's maternal grandmother.[^cite_note-398] On 9 May, Russia fired three [Kinzhal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kh-47M2_Kinzhal) missiles to [Odesa Oblast](61438266.md). At that time, President of the European Council [Charles Michel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Michel) and Prime Minister of Ukraine [Denys Shmyhal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denys_Shmyhal) were in Odesa and had to hide in a bomb shelter. In the evening of the same day, Russian troops fired rockets at three warehouses in Odesa and a shopping centre in the village of Fontanka near the city. One person was killed and two were injured in the warehouses. Three people were also injured in the mall.[^cite_note-399]&#x20;

On 18 November 2024, Russia struck residential buildings and civilian infrastructure in Odesa, resulting in 10 deaths and 39 injuries, including a child, according to the head of the Odesa Regional Military Administration.[^cite_note-w873-400]&#x20;

On 10 June 2025, Russian drones struck medical facilities, including a maternity ward, as well as residential buildings, with officials saying the attacks resulted in two people dying and 13 being injured.[^cite_note-d385-314][^cite_note-c751-401]&#x20;

On 22 July 2025, Odesa was targeted by more than 10 drones according to the Southern Air Command. The attack resulted in damaged civilian infrastructure and one woman sustaining a head injury, with local emergency services saying she had been hospitalized.[^cite_note-t029-180]&#x20;

### Serhiivka missile strike



Aftermath of the Serhiivka missile strike, July 2022

Main article: [Serhiivka missile strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serhiivka_missile_strike)

According to preliminary information, on the morning of 1 July 2022 at 01\:00 AM ([UTC+3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UTC%2B3)), three [Tu-22M3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tu-22M3) strategic bombers of the Russian Air Force flew from the [Volgograd Oblast](478748.md) to [Crimea](163045.md)[^cite_note-402] and, after 1,200 kilometres (750 mi), fired three [Kh-22s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kh-22), supersonic anti-ship missiles designed for use against aircraft carriers, into a 9-storey apartment building and a recreational center in the settlement of [Serhiivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serhiivka,_Serhiivka_settlement_hromada,_Bilhorod-Dnistrovskyi_Raion,_Odesa_Oblast), [Bilhorod-Dnistrovskyi Raion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bilhorod-Dnistrovskyi_Raion), [Odesa Oblast](61438266.md).[^cite_note-403] A missile hit the apartment, and one section of the building was completely destroyed. The fire also spread from the apartment building to an attached store. At least 16 Ukrainian civilians were killed in the residential building. Two missiles hit the recreational center, killing at least 5 (including a 12-year-old boy).[^cite_note-bbc-239] 38 more were also wounded, including 6 children.[^cite_note-bbc-239]&#x20;

2 July was declared a day of mourning in the region.[^cite_note-USI-404] Ukrainian President [Zelenskyy](59210523.md) accused Russia of having committed "an act of conscious, deliberately targeted Russian terror—and not some kind of mistake."[^cite_note-405] He noted that as in the recent Kremenchuk shopping mall attack, the Russian army "used unnecessarily powerful weapons to strike a civilian object."[^cite_note-\:1a-406][^cite_note-\:2-407]&#x20;

A spokesman for the Russian presidency, [Dmitry Peskov](9214683.md), denied that Russia was attacking civilian objects in Ukraine and said that the targeted buildings were used for military purposes.[^cite_note-\:1a-406][^cite_note-\:2-407] [Amnesty International](18947898.md) visited the locations and studied satellite imagery, finding no evidence that the targeted buildings were used by the military.[^cite_note-\:1a-406][^cite_note-408] Official representative of Germany [Steffen Hebestreit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Steffen_Hebestreit\&action=edit\&redlink=1) described the missile strike as an "inhumane and cynical" [war crime](48626.md).[^cite_note-409]&#x20;

### December 2025 attacks

On 18 December 2025, the head of Odesa Oblast Military Administration, [Oleh Kiper](77737641.md), said that a Russian drone struck a civilian car that was driving across a bridge in Odesa; the driver of the car was killed while her three children that were in the car were injured. Kiper called the attack a "cynical war crime."[^cite_note-h523-410] On 19 December, a Russian ballistic missile attack on port infrastructure killed at least eight people and wounded 27. The port was struck again the next day, and Ukrainian officials said that the bombardment was part of a Russian campaign against Odesa's civilian infrastructure amid freezing winter temperatures.[^cite_note-h424-411]&#x20;

### January 2026 attacks

On 27 January 2026, officials said that a Russian drone attack in Odesa had killed at least three people and wounded 23, with a pregnant woman and two children among the injured. The drones hit apartment blocks and also targeted the power grid with the coldest winter in years ongoing.[^cite_note-y852-412]&#x20;

### February 2026 attacks

On the eve of 23 February 2026, the fourth anniversary of the war, Russian forces launched a wave of missile and drone strikes across Ukraine, killing four people, including two civilians in Odesa.[^cite_note-\:1-413] Governor Oleh Kiper announced via Telegram that the drone attack damaged production and warehouse facilities, administrative buildings, car dealership premises, and vehicles.[^cite_note-\:1-413]&#x20;

### March 2026 attacks

On 28 March 2026, Odesa was targeted during a Russian drone barrage across the country, with regional officials reporting three deaths, one 76-year-old man later dying of his injuries in the hospital, and 14 injured, including a nine-year-old boy, and a maternity hospital being damaged.[^cite_note-e762-414][^cite_note-v009-415][^cite_note-b779-416]&#x20;

### April 2026 attacks

On 6 April 2026, regional governor Kiper said that a Russian drone attack striking residential buildings, energy infrastructure, and a kindergarten resulted in the deaths of three people, including a mother and her two-year-old daughter, also injuring 16 people, including a pregnant woman and two children.[^cite_note-t513-417]&#x20;

On 13–14 April 2026, [Izmail port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Izmail) in Southern Odesa was attacked by Russian drones, striking two foreign civilian vessels. According to Ukraine's Deputy Prime Minister [Oleksiy Kuleba](70796239.md), a [Panama](22997.md)-flagged vessel was damaged, along with local infrastructure and equipment. In a separate strike, the country's [Ministry for Development of Communities and Territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Communities_and_Territories_Development) reported that a [Liberian](17791.md)-flagged civilian merchant vessel on its way to the port of [Chornomorsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chornomorsk) was hit, resulting in a fire. Named *Lady Maris* by the [Ukrainian navy](1590554.md), the vessel was traveling through the maritime corridor to load corn. The fire was immediately extinguished by its crew, and the vessel reached a port safely. In other parts of the region, governor Oleh Kiper reported that there were also strikes where a car repair shop was destroyed, triggering a fire that consumed two passenger buses and seven cars. Meanwhile, six private buildings suffered damage to their roofs, ⁠and an ambulance was also harmed. Additionally, regional prosecutors later stated that a 51-year-old man was hospitalized due to the attack.[^cite_note-418]&#x20;

### June 2026 attack on beach

On 23 June 2026, regional governor Kiper said that a 26-year-old woman had been killed by a Russian drone attack that also injured one more person. According to local Telegram channels, the woman was struck by shrapnel near the beach.[^cite_note-e798-419][^cite_note-v277-420]&#x20;

### July 2026 strike

On 17 July 2026, Kiper said that two people had been killed and at least 10 injured in a Russian strike. One of the people killed was a [Red Cross](15487.md) volunteer and first-aid instructor, who was heading to a shelter with her children when she was killed.[^cite_note-g420-40]&#x20;

### August 2026 strike

On 9 August 2026, the Ukrainian Minister of Internal Affairs [Ivan Vyhivskyi](74353482.md) said that a bus carrying 12 children had been damaged by a blast wave during repeated Russian daytime shelling. No serious injuries were reported.[^cite_note-s003-421]&#x20;

## Poltava Oblast

### Kremenchuk shopping mall attack

Main article: [Kremenchuk shopping mall attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kremenchuk_shopping_mall_attack)



Shopping center in Kremenchuk after the shelling on 27 June 2022

On 27 June 2022, the Russian Armed Forces fired two Kh-22 anti-ship missiles into central [Kremenchuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kremenchuk), [Poltava Oblast](1567650.md), hitting the [Amstor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amstor) [shopping mall](235723.md). A fire broke out, and, according to [Dmytro Lunin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmytro_Lunin), [Governor of Poltava Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_Poltava_Oblast), the attack killed at least 20 people and injured at least 56.[^cite_note-bbckremenchuk-422] 36 people were also initially reported missing.[^cite_note-423] According to the Ukrainian Armed Forces, the attack was carried out by Russian Tu-22M3 strategic bombers that took off from the [Shaykovka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaykovka) air base in the [Kaluga Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaluga_Oblast). The missiles were launched over the territory of the [Kursk Oblast](480916.md).[^cite_note-bbckremenchuk-422] Ukrainian Interior Minister Denys Monastyrsky said that the missile hit the far end of the shopping mall.[^cite_note-424] The area of the resulting fire was more than 10,000 square metres (110,000 sq ft), and up to 115 firefighters and 20 fire-fighting appliances were involved in extinguishing it.[^cite_note-bbckremenchuk-422]&#x20;

On the day of the attack, Russian television did not report it until the Russian Ministry of Defense confirmed that it had happened.[^cite_note-TG-425] Pro-Russian Telegram channels have spread multiple conflicting theories about the missile strike, including the claim that the missile was aimed at a car factory near the mall; that the mall was being used as a military equipment warehouse or as a base of the Territorial Defense Forces; and that the missile strike is a Ukrainian provocation involving the use of "canned bodies."[^cite_note-426] On the day after the attack, Russian authorities and state-controlled media issued a number of contradictory statements about the attack, including claims that the attack was "fake" and that the Ukrainian army had bombed the mall themselves.[^cite_note-\:1a-406] Russian Defense Ministry spokesman Igor Konashenkov said, "The detonation of the munitions for Western weaponry in storage led to a fire in a non-functioning shopping centre next to the factory."[^cite_note-Seddon2020628-427] The claims that the shopping mall was "non-functional" have been debunked by several organizations.[^cite_note-\:bbckremenchuk1-428] According to Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelenskyy, there were more than 1,000 people inside the mall when the strike occurred.[^cite_note-Reuters_Kremenchuk_27Jun2022-429] The non-profit online journalism collective [Bellingcat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bellingcat) used receipts from recent purchases at the mall to prove that the mall had been open prior to the attack.[^cite_note-Tondo-430] The BBC also published interviews with people who were working or shopping in the mall at the time.[^cite_note-431] Per reports from independent military experts and researchers with Molfar, a global open-source intelligence community, the factory and mall were too far apart from one another to cause any fires or explosions.[^cite_note-Tondo-430]&#x20;

The leaders of the [G7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_of_Seven) nations,[^cite_note-432] US Secretary of State Antony Blinken[^cite_note-433] British Prime Minister Boris Johnson,[^cite_note-434] Ukrainian president Volodymyr Zelenskyy,[^cite_note-435] Ukraine's Foreign Minister Dmytro Kuleba[^cite_note-\:0-436] and Mayor [Vitalii Maletskyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Vitalii_Maletskyi\&action=edit\&redlink=1) described the attack as "[war crimes](48626.md)," "[crimes against humanity](61099.md)," and "[indiscriminate attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiscriminate_attack)", as well as claiming that the attack was intentional and that there were "no close military targets."[^cite_note-437]&#x20;

### February 2025 Poltava missile strike

Main article: [February 2025 Poltava strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/February_2025_Poltava_strike)



Aftermath of the 1 February 2025 Poltava missile strike

On 1 February 2025, Russia struck a residential building in Poltava, which emergency services said killed 14 people, including two children.[^cite_note-i925-438][^cite_note-z049-439]&#x20;

### July 2026 drone strike

On 30 July 2026, Poltava's regional head Vitalii Diakivnych said that one person had been killed in a drone attack.[^cite_note-r672-344]&#x20;

## Rivne missile strikes

Main article: [Rivne strikes (2022–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rivne_strikes_\(2022%E2%80%93present\))

On 14 March 2022, Russian troops carried out two airstrikes against the [Rivnenska TV Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Rivnenska_TV_Tower\&action=edit\&redlink=1), as a result of which 21 people were killed and 9 were injured.[^cite_note-440] Rockets hit the television tower and administrative buildings nearby.[^cite_note-441] On 25 June, a rocket attack was carried out on civilian infrastructure in the city of Sarny. At least four people were killed and seven others were injured.[^cite_note-442] On the morning of 22 October 2022, Russian troops launched a missile attack on energy infrastructure; as a result of the attack, electric substations were damaged.[^cite_note-443] There were no casualties.[^cite_note-444]&#x20;

## Sumy Oblast

See also: [Russian occupation of Sumy Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_occupation_of_Sumy_Oblast)

### Okhtyrka school bombing



Civilians killed in a Russian cluster bombing of Okhtyrka, 25 February 2022

On 25 February 2022, Russian cluster bombs from a 220 mm [BM-27 Uragan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BM-27_Uragan) rocket hit a preschool in [Okhtyrka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Okhtyrka) used as a civilian bomb shelter, killing three people, including a child. [UAV](58900.md) film showed four hits on the roof of the preschool, three on the ground next to the school, two injured or dead civilians, and pools of blood. Amnesty International analysed 65 photos and videos of the event and interviewed local residents.[^cite_note-Amnesty_UA_cluster_munitions_kill_child-445] *Bellingcat* stated that remains of the [9M27K rocket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BM-27_Uragan#Rockets) were found 200 metres east of the kindergarten. Russian forces were located west of Okhtyrka. Amnesty described the rocket type as "unguided and notoriously inaccurate" and described the attack as potentially a war crime that should be investigated.[^cite_note-Amnesty_UA_cluster_munitions_kill_child-445]&#x20;

### Sumy airstrikes and drone strikes

See also: [Battle of Sumy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Sumy)



Aftermath of the Russian missile strike on Sumy, 13 April 2025, which killed 35 people

In the evening and throughout the night on 7 March 2022, Russian forces executed an airstrike on Sumy's residential neighbourhood. About 22 people were killed, including three children.[^cite_note-446][^cite_note-447] Under the procedural guidance of the Sumy District Prosecutor's Office, criminal proceedings have been instituted for violating the laws and customs of war.[^cite_note-448]&#x20;

On 19 September 2024, a Russian guided aerial bomb struck a five-story [nursing home](430716.md) for the elderly in Sumy, killing at least one and injuring 12, according to the Ukrainian Interior Ministry. The oblast had been hit regularly by Russian shelling and airstrikes since the beginning of the invasion in 2022, but Ukraine's [offensive into the Kursk Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Kursk_offensive) brought the front line closer and led to an increase in attacks by Russia. The energy infrastructure in particular had been badly damaged in Sumy, as part of a broader Russian campaign to degrade Ukraine's power plants and make it more difficult for utilities to provide basic services.[^cite_note-j069-449]&#x20;

The United Nations Human Rights Monitoring Mission said in a September 2024 report that large-scale Russian attacks on Ukrainian energy infrastructure "have inflicted extensive harm and hardship on the country's civilian population, with potentially devastating consequences as winter approaches."[^cite_note-j069-449]&#x20;

On 28 September 2024, Ukrainian officials reported that at least eight people had died following two consecutive Russian drone attacks on a medical centre in Sumy. Interior Minister Klymenko said that the first attack in the morning killed one person, and it was followed by another attack while patients and staff were evacuating. Regional prosecutors said there were 86 patients and 38 staff in the medical centre at the time of the attack.[^cite_note-k738-450] The method of hitting a target once and then striking the same target again as first responders arrive is known as a "[double tap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Double_tap_strike)". Russia has been accused of carrying out strikes of this type during its invasion of Ukraine.[^cite_note-r756-451]&#x20;

On 18 November 2024, a Russian strike devastated a residential building in Sumy, with 11 deaths, including two children, and 89 wounded reported by Ukraine's Emergencies Ministry. Interior Minister Ihor Klymenko also said the attack left Sumy's administrative center without power.[^cite_note-w873-400]&#x20;

On 22 July 2025, Russian forces struck residential buildings in Sumy, injuring at least 12 people.[^cite_note-t029-180]&#x20;

On 4 April 2026, the regional head [Oleh Hryhorov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleh_Hryhorov) said that one person had been killed after a Russian guided aerial bomb hit an apartment block.[^cite_note-l863-331]&#x20;

On 27 June 2026, Hryhorov said that a 66-year-old man was killed as a result of a Russian drone attack on a house, while adding that further attacks in the region injured 10 people.[^cite_note-t265-74]&#x20;

On 3 July 2026, regional officials and Ukraine's state emergency service said that a Russian guided bomb and drone attack on Sumy resulted in three people being killed, including a five-year-old child and her mother, while 27 people were reported injured, with children also among the injured.[^cite_note-p389-452][^cite_note-g278-453][^cite_note-n630-454]&#x20;

On 17 July 2026, local authorities said that Russian attacks injured 10 people across three Sumy communities, including a four-year-old girl.[^cite_note-g420-40]&#x20;

On 5 August 2026, [Suspilne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suspilne) said that Russian guided bomb strikes had destroyed two houses on the night of 3–4 August, killing a 76-year-old woman in one house and two siblings aged five and ten in the other, where their mother managed to pull out the father from the rubble.[^cite_note-h487-455][^cite_note-v014-456]&#x20;

On 20 September 2026, *Reuters* reported a Russian strike close to an apartment building in Sumy, killing one person and wounding another.[^cite_note-d109-208]&#x20;

#### Palm Sunday missile strike on Sumy

Main article: [2025 Sumy airstrike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Sumy_airstrike)

On 13 April 2025, Russian missiles struck Sumy residents gathering for Sunday church services, killing at least 32 people, including two children, and wounding at least 99 people, including 11 children according to the Interior Ministry of Ukraine. Ukrainian officials said that the attack was carried out using two ballistic missiles, and cluster munitions were used in order to kill as many civilians as possible. According to Ukraine's Economy Minister [Yulia Svyrydenko](69191807.md), the attack was carried out on one of the busiest church-going days of the year. [CNN](62028.md) verified social media footage of the attack. The attack was the deadliest single attack on Ukrainian civilians since 2023, when 51 civilians were killed in strikes on Kupiansk. The attack was internationally condemned, with the Trump administration's special envoy to Ukraine and Russia, [Keith Kellogg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keith_Kellogg), commenting on the attack, saying it ″crosses any line of decency.″ The European Union's foreign policy chief, [Kaja Kallas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaja_Kallas), also commented, calling the strike a "horrific example of Russia intensifying attacks while Ukraine has accepted an unconditional ceasefire," while French President [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) said "strong measures" are needed for a ceasefire. Military expert Joakim Paasikivi referred to the strikes as an "obvious war crime," noting the usage of ballistic missiles on the city center of Sumy and the amount of killed and wounded.[^cite_note-n557-457][^cite_note-h183-458][^cite_note-j877-459]&#x20;

#### Killing of couple fleeing village

On 27 January 2025, a couple was killed by Russian forces as they were trying to evacuate from a [Krasnopillia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krasnopillia,_Sumy_Oblast) village in Sumy. According to regional head Oleh Hryhorov, the man was trying to pull his injured wife on a [sled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sled) towards a safer area before a drone first struck and killed his injured wife and a second [FPV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FPV_drone) killed the man.[^cite_note-c598-460][^cite_note-b721-461]&#x20;

#### Repeated drone strikes on shopping center

On 9 September 2026, Hryhorov said that a Russian drone struck a Sumy shopping center, killing two people and injuring 20 as a fire broke out and the building was badly damaged. The next day, the city administration said the building had been struck again by another Russian drone while it was closed for repairs. No casualties were reported in the second strike, though it caused another fire.[^cite_note-b722-462][^cite_note-a146-463]&#x20;

## Uman apartment block strike

Main article: [2023 Uman missile strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Uman_missile_strike)



Aftermath of the Uman apartment block strike

On 28 April 2023, Russian forces launched 23 [cruise missiles](6590.md) and two [suicide drones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One-way_attack_drone) at targets across Ukraine. Two missiles hit an apartment block in [Uman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uman), killing 23 people, including four children.[^cite_note-464] Nine other residential buildings were damaged in the city. Shortly after, the Russian Defense Ministry posted a photo of a missile launch on its Telegram channel with the caption "Right on target." The message outraged Ukrainians, who saw it as gloating over the casualties. The Russian Defense Ministry said it had targeted Ukrainian army reserve units with the strikes.[^cite_note-465] Power grid operator [Ukrenergo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrenergo) said the missile barrage did not damage the country's energy infrastructure.[^cite_note-466]&#x20;

## Vinnytsia missile strike



Aftermath of the Vinnytsia missile strike, 14 July 2022

Main article: [Vinnytsia strikes (2022–present) § July 2022 rocket attack on the city centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vinnytsia_strikes_\(2022%E2%80%93present\)#July_2022_rocket_attack_on_the_city_centre)

At about 10\:10 AM on 14 July 2022, an air raid alarm sounded in the city. At approximately 10\:42, local residents reported three explosions in the city. Before that, local residents noticed a missile flying over [Bershad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bershad) city and Vinnytsia. According to Ukrainian authorities, the Russian Naval Forces fired five [Kalibr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalibr) cruise missiles from a submarine in the [Black Sea](3386.md). Ukraine claims that two of the missiles were shot down.[^cite_note-ap_2022-07-14-467] One of the missiles reportedly hit the [House of Officers](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=House_of_Officers\&action=edit\&redlink=1), a Soviet-era concert hall.[^cite_note-468] But, according to Ukrainian officials, two missiles also struck civilian buildings, including a medical center, offices, stores, and residential buildings in the center of the city.[^cite_note-469] The attacks killed at least 28 people (including three children) and injured at least 202 others.[^cite_note-BBCvinnytsia-470][^cite_note-471]&#x20;

Local officials pointed out that Kalibr missiles are high-precision, which indicates that the Russians purposefully targeted civilians.[^cite_note-ap_2022-07-14-467] The strike has been labeled as a war crime by officials from multiple countries.[^cite_note-472][^cite_note-473] Ukrainian president [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md) wrote on his Telegram channel: "Vinnytsia. Missile strikes in the city centre. There are wounded and killed, among them a little child. Every day, Russia destroys the civilian population, kills Ukrainian children, and directs rockets at civilian objects. Where there is nothing military. What is this if not an open terrorist attack? Inhuman. Country of killers. A country of terrorists".[^cite_note-474][^cite_note-475] The strike has also been labeled as a war crime by Ukrainian Interior Minister [Denys Monastyrsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denys_Monastyrsky).[^cite_note-BBCvinnytsia-470]&#x20;

The [Ministry of Defense of Russia](7875884.md) officially recognized the attack on [Vinnytsia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vinnytsia) the next day, saying that they hit the garrison house of officers, where allegedly "...a meeting of the command of the [Ukrainian Air Force](1135769.md) with representatives of foreign arms suppliers was taking place..." According to them, most participants of the meeting were killed.[^cite_note-476] Among the dead were allegedly three officers of the Air Force of Ukraine.[^cite_note-477][^cite_note-478] The missile strike occurred during a [conference in The Hague](14918.md) on holding Russia accountable for war crimes.[^cite_note-479] The ambassador of Moldova to Ukraine, [Valeriu Chiveri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valeriu_Chiveri), condemned the attack on Vinnytsia, referring to attacks on civilian targets in Ukrainian cities away from the frontlines as crimes against humanity. He also mentioned the [European Union](9317.md)'s decision to grant candidate status to both Moldova and Ukraine and talked about the need for both countries to work together.[^cite_note-480]&#x20;

## Zaporizhzhia Oblast

### Bombing of Zaporizhzhia

Main article: [Zaporizhzhia strikes (2022–present)](72165443.md)



Paramedics helping a victim of a Russian guided bomb strike on Zaporizhzhia, 8 January 2025, which killed 13 people

One civilian was killed and two others injured when five Russian shells were fired at Zaporizhzhia at 7.15pm on 12 August 2022. Further city infrastructure in the Shevchenkivskyi District was also damaged in the shelling.[^cite_note-481]&#x20;

During the night of 19 September 2022, Zaporizhzhia was hit by eight Russian rockets in its industrial and residential areas. Followed by another rocket attack in the morning, striking the regional center near the Dnieper river.[^cite_note-rbc.ua-482] Two days later the city was again hit by two Russian rockets during the night, followed by another five rockets attacks in the daytime. The regional center was hit an additional two times while other infrastructure and residential houses were damaged, two of the projectiles landed in a field on the outskirts of the city. The attack wounded three civilians.[^cite_note-rbc.ua-482] The following day on 22 September, nine more rockets were fired at the city. One of the projectiles hit a hotel in the city's central park, killing one civilian and injuring five others. An electrical substation and several high-rise residential buildings were also damaged. Later that same day, ten more rockets struck the city and damaged about a dozen private homes.[^cite_note-483]&#x20;

At 5.08 am on 6 October 2022, seven Russian rockets were fired towards the city center of Zaporizhzhia. Several residential buildings were destroyed, and fires broke out due to the attack, killing 17 civilians and injuring 12 more.[^cite_note-484][^cite_note-485] Zaporizhzhia was attacked once more during the night of 7 October, but this time by [Iranian](14653.md) [Shahed-136](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahed-136) [kamikaze drones](53703349.md) used by the Russian forces. The attack resulted in the deaths of 12 civilians with a further 13 injured and 15 missing.[^cite_note-486][^cite_note-487]&#x20;

Around 3 am on 9 October 2022, 12 Russian tactical missiles were launched against civilian infrastructure in Zaporizhzhia. Most missiles hit both high-rise buildings and residential houses, with a nine-story building being partially destroyed after the attack. A further five high-rise buildings, 20 residential houses, and four schools were damaged alongside 20 cars. A total of 13 civilians were killed in the attack, while 89 more were injured.[^cite_note-488] The following day at 1.45 am, about seven Russian S-300 anti-aircraft missiles struck the city, resulting in the deaths of eight civilians.[^cite_note-suspilne.media-2022-10-10-489] On 23 November, Russian missile strikes destroyed a maternity ward in Ukraine's Zaporizhzhia Oblast, in the town of Vilnyansk, killing a newborn baby.[^cite_note-490]&#x20;

On 10 October 2022, at 1.45 am, about seven Russian S-300 anti-aircraft missiles struck the city, resulting in the deaths of eight civilians.[^cite_note-suspilne.media-2022-10-10-489] Later on the day, an apartment block was destroyed and a kindergarten was damaged by shelling.[^cite_note-491][^cite_note-492][^cite_note-493] 5 people were killed and 8 were injured in that shelling.[^cite_note-494][^cite_note-rfi_2022_10_11-495]&#x20;

On 11 November 2024, an overnight glide bomb attack on Zaporizhzhia damaged a dormitory and partially destroyed a two-story apartment building. Ukraine's police said that one person was killed and 21 injured, including a four-year-old.[^cite_note-q973-496]&#x20;

On 6 December 2024, a car shop in Zaporizhzhia was struck by Russia. Regional officials said that 10 people were killed and 24 wounded, including two children.[^cite_note-b667-82]&#x20;

On 10 December 2024, local officials said a private [clinic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clinic) and residential buildings in Zaporizhzhia were hit by a ballistic missile, most likely an Iskander, killing at least eight people and injuring another 22, including a child.[^cite_note-q468-497]&#x20;

On 8 January 2025, Russian troops launched glide bombs at Zaporizhzhia in the afternoon, with at least two bombs striking residential buildings according to the regional governor [Ivan Fedorov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivan_Fedorov_\(politician\)).[^cite_note-s639-498] The following day, the death toll was 13 people and the amount of injured around 113.[^cite_note-q380-499]&#x20;

On 5 October 2025, Fedorov said one person had been killed and 10 wounded by overnight Russian attacks that also left more than 73,000 people without power after a power plant was struck.[^cite_note-v732-383][^cite_note-c124-384]&#x20;

On 10 October 2025, a child was reported killed while several others, including his parents, were wounded as Russia targeted Zaporizhzhia residential areas and energy sites with drones, guided bombs, and missiles.[^cite_note-r538-321][^cite_note-t878-322][^cite_note-d067-323]&#x20;

On 3 April 2026, local authorities in [Nikopol](1958336.md) said that five people had been killed and 27 wounded in Russian overnight air raids.[^cite_note-l863-331] On 7 April, Dnipropetrovsk governor Oleksandr Ganzha said that Russian FPV drones struck buses in the region, with one hitting a bus approaching Nikopol's city center, killing four people and injuring at least 16. A second bus was hit in a neighboring community, injuring five people.[^cite_note-q38f-500]&#x20;

#### Zaporizhzhia civilian convoy attack



Aftermath of the Zaporizhzhia civilian convoy attack, September 2022

Main article: [Zaporizhzhia civilian convoy attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zaporizhzhia_civilian_convoy_attack)

On 30 September 2022, a Russian [S-300 missile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S-300_missile_system) hit a civilian convoy of civilian cars near [Zaporizhzhia](1873579.md), killing 32 people, including a three-month-old child.[^cite_note-radiosvoboda_2022_10_08-501] and injuring around 88.[^cite_note-502] People in cars had gathered in a logistic hub to register for entering Russian-occupied territories in the south, such as the cities of [Mariupol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariupol) and [Melitopol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melitopol), and they were planning either to return home or to meet relatives and take them back to government-controlled territory. According to a spokesperson for the local governor's office, the attack on civilians was deliberate, as no military objective was placed near the site.[^cite_note-503][^cite_note-504] It occurred hours before Russia formally [annexed four oblasts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_annexation_of_Donetsk,_Kherson,_Luhansk_and_Zaporizhzhia_oblasts) of Ukraine, including [Zaporizhzhia Oblast](494936.md).[^cite_note-505]&#x20;

#### Zaporizhzhia apartment block strike



Aftermath of the Zaporizhzhia apartment block strike

Main article: [2022 Zaporizhzhia residential building airstrike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Zaporizhzhia_residential_building_airstrike)

On 9 October 2022, 3 a.m. (UTC+3\:00), six missiles were launched at a residential area in Zaporizhzhia, destroying an apartment building and damaging 70 other buildings. The attack resulted in the deaths of 13 people, including a child.[^cite_note-cnn1-506] Another 89 were injured, 11 of whom are children.[^cite_note-cnn1-506] The missiles reportedly originated from Russian-controlled locations in Zaporizhzhia.[^cite_note-507] The airstrike took place the day after [an explosion](71948884.md) damaged large parts of the [Crimean Bridge](22563317.md), which [Russian president](239646.md) [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) accused Ukraine of carrying out and called it an "act of terrorism."[^cite_note-508]&#x20;

### Orikhiv aid center bombing



Aid center destroyed by a Russian bomb on 9 July 2023

On 9 July 2023, a Russian [guided bomb](3587974.md) struck a school being used to distribute [humanitarian aid](434679.md) in the town of [Orikhiv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orikhiv), Zaporizhzhia Oblast. Seven people were killed and eleven wounded. The General Prosecutor's office said the attack was being investigated as a war crime.[^cite_note-509]&#x20;

### Malokaterynivka strike near children's playground

On 21 August 2024, a 14-year-old boy died following a Russian munition hitting a kiosk a few meters away from a children's playground in [Malokaterynivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malokaterynivka), southern Zaporizhzhia. The Ukrainian police stated that four children ranging in age from 11 to 17 had been injured in the attack.[^cite_note-z000-510]&#x20;

### Bilenke prison bombing

On 28 July 2025, Ukraine's Penitentiary Service said that Russian strikes targeted the [Bilenke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bilenke,_Zaporizhzhia_Oblast) penitentiary, killing at least 17 and wounding 82. Buildings within the penitentiary were destroyed, and adjacent private homes were damaged in the attack.[^cite_note-k348-58][^cite_note-k906-248]&#x20;

### June 2026 attack

On 27 June 2026, Ukraine's state emergency service said that Russian attacks on Zaporizhzhia resulted in nine injuries, including three children.[^cite_note-t265-74]&#x20;

### July 2026 attacks

On 6 July 2026, regional officials and Ukraine's emergency services said that two people had been killed and nine injured, including a child, when Russian drones attacked filling stations across the region.[^cite_note-h278-511]&#x20;

On 17 July, local authorities said that Russia carried out 853 strikes across the region, hitting 58 settlements and killing three people and injuring five, including four children.[^cite_note-g420-40]&#x20;

On 27 July, Fedorov said that a 67-year-old man was killed and seven people injured by Russian airstrikes.[^cite_note-o640-512]&#x20;

### August 2026 double-tap strike

On 9 August 2026, Minister Vyhivskyi said that a firefighter was killed while two other emergency workers were injured in a Russian double-tap strike.[^cite_note-c139-513]&#x20;

## Zakarpattia Oblast

On 21 August 2025, Ukrainian officials said that 15 people were injured when Russian cruise missiles struck an American factory in [Mukachevo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mukachevo), [Zakarpattia Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zakarpattia_Oblast), producing household goods.[^cite_note-l839-382][^cite_note-e580-514]&#x20;

## Bombing of Zhytomyr

Main article: [Zhytomyr attacks (2022–present)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhytomyr_attacks_\(2022%E2%80%93present\))



Emergency servicemen carry a dead body found under rubble in [Malyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malyn) city, [Zhytomyr Oblast](1567596.md), after a Russian airstrike on 8 March 2022.

On 1 March 2022, late in the evening, Russian troops hit a residential sector of the city. About 10 residential buildings on [Shukhevych](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Shukhevych) Street and around the city hospital were damaged. A few bombs were dropped on the city. As a result, at least two Ukrainian civilians were killed and three were injured.[^cite_note-515] On 2 March, shells hit the regional perinatal center and some private houses.[^cite_note-516] On 4 March, rockets hit the 25th Zhytomyr school, destroying half of the school.[^cite_note-517] On 8 March, in an air assault, a dormitory was hit.[^cite_note-518] On 9 March, the outskirts of the city (Ozerne district) came under fire.[^cite_note-519]&#x20;

## Ternopil Oblast

Main article: [2025 Ternopil strike](81637747.md)



Aftermath of the Russian airstrike on Ternopil, 19 November 2025, which killed 36 people

On 19 November 2025, Ukrainian officials said that Russian cruise missiles had struck residential buildings in [Ternopil](512798.md), western Ukraine, killing at least 34 people and wounding over 90 others, with six children being among the dead and 15 children among the wounded. A video shared by Zelenskyy showed one of the residential buildings completely caved in. The attack, which took place close to the Polish border, resulted in NATO scrambling aircraft and Poland temporarily closing two of its airports.[^cite_note-i604-520][^cite_note-s669-521][^cite_note-l529-522]&#x20;

## Cherkasy Oblast

On 14 April 2026, the head of the [Cherkasy Oblast](494894.md) military administration, [Ihor Taburets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ihor_Taburets), said that five people had been killed, including an eight-year-old boy, and 12 people wounded by a Russian drone attack.[^cite_note-u392-523][^cite_note-j511-62]&#x20;

## Incident in Galați, Romania

On 29 May 2026, Romania said that a Russian [Geran-2](71747103.md) drone had struck a residential block in [Galati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gala%C8%9Bi_County), causing a fire that injured two people and required the evacuation of seventy more.[^cite_note-a298-524][^cite_note-a748-525][^cite_note-m323-526] Romania's president [Nicusor Dan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicusor_Dan) said the Russian drone had hit the building by mistake.[^cite_note-nyt_romania_drone-527]&#x20;

## Placement of military objectives near civilian objects

See also: [Stara Krasnianka care house attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stara_Krasnianka_care_house_attack) and [Criticism of Amnesty International § Report on placement of Ukrainian forces in civilian areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criticism_of_Amnesty_International#Report_on_placement_of_Ukrainian_forces_in_civilian_areas)

Both the Russian and the Ukrainian armies have been accused of violating [international humanitarian law](1093604.md) by locating military objectives within densely populated areas without removing civilians to safer areas.[^cite_note-OSCE_Interim_Report-528][^cite_note-HRW_Endangering_Civilians-529] International humanitarian law requires all parties to the conflict to avoid, to the extent feasible, "locating military objectives within or near densely populated areas"[^cite_note-530] and requires them to "remove civilian persons and objects under its control from the vicinity of military objectives."[^cite_note-531] Contrary to the use of [human shields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_shield), these fighting tactics do not involve utilizing the presence of civilians to render certain areas immune from military operations.[^cite_note-532]&#x20;

On 29 June, the [Office of the United Nations High Commissioner for Human Rights](22463.md) expressed concern about Russian and Ukrainian armed forces taking up positions close to civilian objects without taking measures for protecting the civilians. The human rights agency had also received reports of the use of [human shields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_shield).[^cite_note-Report_OHCHR-17]<sup>: para. 34 </sup> OHCHR documented the consequences of these fighting tactics in the case of a [care house in Stara Krasnianka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stara_Krasnianka_care_house_attack), where the Ukrainian army had set up a firing position without first evacuating the residents, and in the case of a school in [Yahidne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yahidne,_Chernihiv_Oblast), where 360 residents, including 74 children were held captive by Russian forces for almost a month.[^cite_note-Report_OHCHR-17]<sup>: para. 34 </sup> Similar concerns were raised also by a report published on 20 July by the [Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe](22796.md)[^cite_note-OSCE_Interim_Report-528] and by [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) on 21 July.[^cite_note-HRW_Endangering_Civilians-529]&#x20;

[Amnesty International](18947898.md) said on 4 August that it had found evidence that Ukrainian forces had repeatedly put civilians in danger by establishing bases and firing positions in populated residential areas, including schools and hospitals; some areas were kilometres away from front lines, and, according to Amnesty International, alternative locations were available to the Ukrainian army.[^cite_note-AI_Fighting_Tactics-533] Between April and July, Amnesty International researchers found evidence that Ukrainian military objectives had been placed within residential areas in 19 towns and villages in the Kharkiv, Donetsk, Luhansk and Mykolaiv oblasts.
