Amnesty International's Secretary General [Agnès Callamard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agn%C3%A8s_Callamard) stated that there was "a pattern of Ukrainian forces putting civilians at risk and violating the laws of war when they operate in populated area."[^cite_note-AI_Fighting_Tactics-533] The Amnesty report sparked significant outrage in Ukraine and the West[^cite_note-534] and [Oksana Pokalchuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oksana_Pokalchuk), head of Amnesty International in Ukraine, resigned from her post and left the organization in protest over the publication of the report.[^cite_note-535] Amnesty's report was criticized by military and legal experts such as John Spencer, a specialist in urban warfare studies, who stated that advising Ukrainian forces not to be in urban areas did not make sense, as the circumstances of the war necessitated that.[^cite_note-536][^cite_note-\:1b-537][^cite_note-538] United Nations war crime investigator Marc Garlasco stated that the Amnesty report got the law wrong and also that Ukraine was making efforts to protect civilians, including helping them to relocate.[^cite_note-\:1b-537]&#x20;

In mid-October 2022, the UN [Independent International Commission of Inquiry on Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_International_Commission_of_Inquiry_on_Ukraine) released a report that included findings that both Russian and Ukrainian forces had "deployed their military assets and troops in ways that can endanger civilians."[^cite_note-539][^cite_note-540]&#x20;

## Analysis



Total number of destroyed or damaged homes in Ukraine by 2025 compared to other modern wars

By January 2025, it is estimated Russia dropped 51,000 [guided bombs](3587974.md) on Ukraine.[^cite_note-541] This is almost three times the amount compared to 19,000 guided bombs dropped by the US and the UK during the [Iraq War](5043324.md).[^cite_note-542] By February 2024, Russia had fired between 12,000,000 and 17,000,000 [artillery shells](337004.md) against Ukraine.[^cite_note-EDM-15] By comparison, the US Army fired 35,000 artillery shells in five months against [Islamic State](9087364.md) in the [2017 intervention in Syria](43683196.md), 60,000 artillery shells in the [Gulf War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_War), and 34,000 artillery shells in the [2003 invasion of Iraq](201936.md).[^cite_note-543]&#x20;

Altogether, 3,404 civilians died in the [War in Donbas (2014–2022)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Donbas_\(2014%E2%80%932022\)).[^cite_note-OHCHR-544] By comparison, after Russia's February 2022 full-scale invasion, 4,163 civilians died in March 2022,[^cite_note-545] meaning that more civilians were killed in that one month alone than in the entire eight years of the Donbas War.&#x20;

The [Uppsala Conflict Data Program](18537936.md) estimated that 95% of casualties in the [siege of Mariupol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Mariupol) were civilians—a [civilian casualty ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civilian_casualty_ratio) higher than the one in the [Gaza War](74998519.md) (83%) and the [Srebrenica massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Srebrenica_massacre) (92%) and surpassed in modern times only by the [Rwandan genocide](676659.md).[^cite_note-theguardian20250821-546]&#x20;

No region in Ukraine was spared from Russian attacks. According to a Ukrainian estimate, only 3% of all Russian missiles, drones, and bombs hit military targets, while 97% hit civilian targets.[^cite_note-UN_Press_11042024-547] By June 2023, [UNDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Development_Programme) estimated 1.5 million homes in Ukraine were either damaged or destroyed in the Russo-Ukrainian War.[^cite_note-UNDP-548] By comparison, approximately 2 million homes were damaged or destroyed in Ukraine during [World War II](32927.md).[^cite_note-549]&#x20;

The [Kyiv School of Economics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyiv_School_of_Economics) estimated in January 2024 that the total area of damaged or destroyed [housing units](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housing_unit) in Ukraine caused by the war was 88,900,000 square metres (88.9 km<sup>2</sup>) or 8.6% of the country's housing stock. This is 1.4 million households, leaving 3.4 million people homeless.[^cite_note-550] Since 2022, half of all pregnancies in Ukraine resulted in [preterm births](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preterm_birth) due to war-related stress.[^cite_note-551] By December 2024, the UN recorded 1,614 attacks on Ukrainian schools by Russian forces.[^cite_note-552]&#x20;

In 2024, [UNICEF](19698110.md) reported that 16 Ukrainian children were killed or injured every week in the war.[^cite_note-553] UNICEF also estimates that 1.5 million Ukrainian children suffered from [PTSD](82974.md) or other mental health problems related to the conflict. On average, Ukrainian children were hiding in underground bomb shelters for 920 hours (more than a month) in 2022. According to a study, trauma exposure in the first year of war encompassed 57% of children aged 7–17, and 45% of children aged 3–6.[^cite_note-554] By 2025, approximately 70% of Ukrainian children, or 3.5 million, were experiencing material deprivation such as lack of food, clothing, heating at home, and access to education—an almost fourfold rise from only 18% who experienced this in 2021. Due to Russian attacks on infrastructure, one in three Ukrainian children lived without water supply or an operative sewage system at home.[^cite_note-555] By February 2026, Russian attacks had killed more than 15,000 civilians according to *[ReliefWeb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ReliefWeb)*.[^cite_note-d689-23]&#x20;
